# Changelog

All notable changes to PhotoCast are documented here.
Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed — the frontend lint gate now fails on warnings

- **`npm run lint` carried no `--max-warnings`**, so every warning-level rule was advisory: the build stayed green with 34 of them outstanding, and CodeQL was the only thing catching the class of defect they described. The gate is now `--max-warnings 0`. It is the same script CI runs, so local and CI tighten together.
- **The 34 were two real gaps, not noise.** 33 were `react/prop-types` on three `HotTopicStrip` components — `AuroraExpandedCard`, `BluebellExpandedCard` and `TideExpandedCard` — which had none while every other component in that file did. The two region-grouped cards take the *same* four props and build an identical map handoff from `topic`, so they share one `EXPANDED_CARD_PROP_TYPES` declaration rather than two hand-copied shapes; a missing key in one copy would only ever downgrade its own warning, which is exactly the drift that stays invisible. `topic` is a `shape` naming the four fields the handoff reads, not the looser `PropTypes.object` used elsewhere in the file — those four are a contract with `onShowOnMap`, and naming them is the documentation.
- The 34th was a **dead prop**: `DailyBriefing` declared and defaulted `homeCoords` and never read it. Removed, along with its `propTypes` entry and the pass-site in `App.jsx`. `App` still holds the state — the map's "centre on home" control uses it.
- Rules stay at `warn` rather than being promoted to `error`. The severity says how bad a thing is; the gate says whether any of them may ship. Conflating the two would make every future rule addition a choice between "blocks the build" and "invisible", which is how 34 accumulated.

### Fixed — the surge chart's unused import, and the magic 24 it should have been

- CodeQL alert 174: `MINUTES_PER_DAY` was imported into `SurgeRunRow` and never used. ESLint did not catch it — unused imports are a *warning* in this config and the gate carries no `--max-warnings`. Deleting the line would have closed the alert and left the actual smell in place: the surge series is indexed by **hour**, so the x-position read `(hour / 24) * VIEW_W` — a bare 24 for the same local day `solarDayGeometry` already describes with a named constant. The import is therefore **swapped rather than removed**: a new `HOURS_PER_DAY` joins its sibling and takes the literal's place. `MINUTES_PER_DAY` keeps its consumers in `TideRunRow`, which samples by minute.

### Changed — the hover peek matches its gesture, and the drill-down stops asking answered questions

- **The "Close to home" peek carried modal weight on an accidental trigger.** Name, stars, drive, the whole generated paragraph and two score bars, summoned by a pointer merely crossing the row — with no delay, no shell, no tether, and a 260px `max-height` with `overflow: hidden` that cut the second score bar in half. It read as the page glitching rather than a panel opening. Hover now gives a **glance**; the click gives the full read in the map overlay that already exists. Nothing left the product: the scores and the whole narrative moved one click away, to the surface that was always their home.
- **Three lines, and the list is closed** — a `★★★★☆` glyph row with `4.0/5` and a drive chip, one clause of the why in the app's serif italic, and `Click for the full read + map →`. Explicitly gone: score bars, the generated-at timestamp, the region line, tide detail, header bar, ✕ and footer. The **name** is gone too, because the arrow tethers the panel to the card that just named it.
- **180ms before it opens, 140ms of grace before it closes.** The pause is the feature — sweeping the whole card row at normal speed now opens nothing. The grace exists because the panel sits 10px *below* the card and the pointer is briefly over neither; the panel's own `mouseenter` cancels it, which is what makes card→panel travel work, and the panel is `pointer-events: auto` so the prompt is clickable. Esc closes it. Both timers are cleared on unmount.
- **Gated on pointer type as well as viewport.** `useIsMobile` asks how wide the viewport is; the new `useIsCoarsePointer` asks what is pointing at it, and the two disagree on exactly the devices that break a hover affordance — a touchscreen laptop or a landscape tablet, where "hover" is a tap that has already committed. Phrased as *is coarse* rather than *can hover* on purpose: an environment that does not implement the query reports no match, which leaves hover behaviour switched **on** rather than silently killing it everywhere the query is unknown.
- **Centred under the card, clamped to the block's own edges, with an 11px arrow at the card's centre.** Still `position: fixed` — the block is `overflow: hidden` and its gold accent rail depends on that to stay inside the rounded corner, so an absolutely-positioned child would be cropped against the card grid, which is the clipping this change exists to remove. It flips above the card when the viewport cannot hold it below. The `max-height` is gone entirely: three short lines need no cap.
- **A missing sentence now means no peek at all.** The rating and the drive both come from the card the pointer is already on, so a peek without the "why" would restate what the reader can see and add a prompt. The forecast rows behind the map are no longer a fallback: they carry scores but no summary, and the scores are no longer part of the peek. `firstClause` truncates at a clause boundary rather than a character count — a spaced dash is a break but `high-cloud` is one word, and the `.` in `0.4 m` does not end a sentence.
- **The Pro gate did not weaken; the content it guarded left the panel.** With no scores on the peek for anyone, an upsell there would advertise something this surface no longer shows either way. The map popup's gate is untouched.

- **Opening the plan→map overlay *is* a filter action** — the user chose the location and the solar event on the way in — so restating it as five rows of controls cost ~300px of map and asked an answered question. In the old build that left roughly **165px** of visible map: too short to show the focused pin, its neighbours and the popup without panning. The overlay now opens on a one-line **context bar**: a `SHOWING` kicker, the inherited event and its clock time as a lead chip, the saved filters that are actually cutting something, a live `N pins in view`, and a `Filters` disclosure at the modal ✕'s weight.
- **The map takes the reclaimed height** — 470px collapsed, 300px with the drawer open, sharing one `.24s` easing so the drawer and the map move as a single gesture. `MapSizeSync` polls `invalidateSize` across the transition rather than firing once at the end, or Leaflet keeps the old geometry for the whole 240ms the user is watching. `MapOverlay`'s map row is now `flex: 0 0 auto` instead of taking the panel's leftover space and clipping the map inside it, and the panel scrolls (`overflow: hidden auto`) rather than cutting its own foot row off on a short viewport.
- **`N pins in view` means the viewport, not the filtered set.** A new `BoundsTracker` reports the map's bounds on `moveend`/`zoomend`, identity-compared before storing so a pan that did not move anything cannot rebuild every marker. Falling back to the full filtered set before the first report is still a true statement; a count that survived a pan would not be.
- **The quality chip is always stated; the rest appear only when they cut.** A `3★+` floor is never a no-op — it is hiding pins right now, and the count beside it would otherwise be unexplained. A drive-time filter left at "any" is cutting nothing, so naming it would pad the receipt with switched-off things.
- **Collapsed every single time, and the choice is not remembered.** A remembered "open" would restate the answered question on every drill-down thereafter. Only the *disclosure* is forgotten — a threshold changed while the drawer is open persists exactly as it does on the Map tab. **The full Map tab is unchanged**: same rail, same primary row, same 500px map, same persisted disclosure, all pinned by tests.
- The drawer's rows are labelled left-to-right from a fixed 78px mono column (`EVENT`, `QUALITY`, `SUBJECT`, `LOGISTICS`) via a shared `FilterRow` — **two layouts, one set of controls**, because a second copy of the quality segment is a second place for "what does this filter do" to drift. The event row is marked `inherited from the plan card`; quality keeps `saved`.

### Added — "Centre on home" on the Map tab

- A real Leaflet control in the top-left stack, in its **own container** 8px under the zoom box rather than as a third button in the zoom bar: recentring is not a zoom step, and putting it in that bar would make it read as one. Leaflet's corner container handles the stacking, so nothing hardcodes a distance from the top of the map.
- **No geocoding on a click.** The postcode was resolved once, server-side, when it was saved; `GET /api/user/settings` returns the stored lat/lon, and those are the same coordinates already driving the Close-to-home radius. A click is a camera move and nothing else.
- **The zoom is derived, not fixed.** "Show me home" means "show me the area I said was local", and that radius is a per-user setting — a hardcoded zoom would frame 30 miles for someone who set 15 and crop someone who set 45. `zoomForHomeRadius` fits the user's own radius into the map's shorter axis and floors the result, so the frame never claims more than it shows. 30 miles is the fallback when no radius has been chosen.
- **With no postcode it stays visible and disabled at 40%**, because a control that is absent explains nothing and this one's whole job when unset is to say where the missing setting lives. It keeps its pointer (a `data-` state, not the `disabled` attribute) and the click opens Settings with the cursor already in the postcode field.
- Absent from the Plan overlay, which is already framed on the spot the user asked about — and whose no-postcode branch would open a settings dialog on top of a modal.
- Three CSS declarations carry a doubled-up selector: `leaflet.css` is imported from `MapView.jsx` and lands *after* `index.css` in the bundle, so `.leaflet-touch .leaflet-bar` and `.leaflet-top .leaflet-control` win on source order at equal specificity. Caught in the browser, not in a test — the control rendered with Leaflet's 4px radius, 2px black border and 10px margin.

### Fixed — a multi-typed location showed no subject icon in the briefing rows

- The briefing drill-down and the heatmap rows indexed `LOCATION_TYPE_ICONS` **with the `locationType` array itself**. A single-typed location got away with it — `['LANDSCAPE']` coerces to the string `'LANDSCAPE'`, which is a real key — but anything with two types produced `'LANDSCAPE,BLUEBELL'`, matched nothing, and rendered no icon at all. So the locations carrying the *most* information about themselves were the only ones showing none of it, which reads as data missing from the location rather than a lookup missing a join. A shared `locationTypeIcons()` now renders every display type, in display order, at all four call sites.
- Drawn from `DISPLAY_TYPES`, so **BLUEBELL stays absent**: it is a seasonal subject, not a kind of place, and a year-round 🌸 would assert a bloom in August. Rannerdale Knotts — the reported case — therefore reads 🏔️, which is also the honest answer to "why is a bluebell site in an August briefing at all": V132 kept its LANDSCAPE typing deliberately (it is an `OPEN_FELL` with a genuine open sightline), so it is a landscape location every day of the year. `BriefingService.isColourLocation` is working exactly as written — a BLUEBELL-**only** site is admitted only inside the bloom window.

### Added — the storm surge pill draws its water, and says what it means first

- **A surge is now a curve, not a number.** The hourly series is computed on the briefing build path from weather already fetched — `StormSurgeService.calculate` is a pure function of pressure, wind speed and wind direction with **no time parameter**, so running it once per local hour costs zero HTTP, zero DB and a few milliseconds across the coastal roster. **No migration.**
- **It rides a carrier, not the briefing payload — and that is not a preference.** Hot topics are recomputed live on every serve: `getCachedBriefing` calls `getHotTopics` unconditionally and rebuilds from the live topics, never from `cached.hotTopics()`. A series written into the persisted JSON would be serialised on the build path and discarded on the first request; worse, because `HotTopic` is a record whose `equals` compares every component, a persisted-but-not-live field makes the cache-equality shortcut fail and **forces** that overwrite. `SurgeCurveService` is an `AtomicReference` carrier, the shape `NlcClarityService` and `MeteorClarityService` already use. The cost is one cycle of durability: after a restart the pill shows its fact chips until the next build, which is the contract those two already accept. A curve is never synthesised to fill the gap.
- **The sentence ships before the picture.** The chart is `aria-hidden`, so the verdict — `+0.72 m at 14:00 · on high water · above predicted tide · at Seaham` — is the entire accessible answer, exists in its own right, and precedes the chart in the DOM. A test pins that order.
- **What the row refuses to say.** Surge riding *on* high water is the case worth the drive, so an unaligned peak reads "high water 21:00, peak misses it" rather than being dressed up. With no derivable high water it states the surge and stops, because an offset against a tide we do not have would be the most confident-sounding sentence on the card and entirely unfounded. Below the significance threshold, or with every hour missing, it builds nothing — a flat line is not a finding.
- **The chart is a sibling of the tide chart, not a mode of it.** `TideRunRow` maps a boolean (`high ? HIGH_Y : LOW_Y`) because it plots shape; surge plots **metres**, so it needs a domain→range scale. That is a different function, not a parameter. Only the pure local-day geometry is shared (`chart/solarDayGeometry.js`); the JSX frame stays duplicated on purpose, so `TideRunRow`'s 12 tests passing **unedited** is the proof the extraction was behaviour-preserving.
- **Drawn as a forecast, beside an almanac.** The trace is dashed because a tide is fixed and knowable while a surge is a weather prediction, and two charts adjacent in one strip must not imply equal confidence. The zero line is the *predicted tide* — no absolute water level is claimed anywhere, because the tide datum itself is undocumented upstream. A **0.5 m domain floor** stops a 0.06 m wobble being auto-scaled into a mountain range: the classic way a chart lies while every number on it stays true.
- **Gaps break the path.** A missing hour is a null, not a zero; a line through it would descend to a value nobody forecast. During BST the first hour of the local day is genuinely absent from the UTC array, and reads as a gap rather than as the hour after it.
- **Coastal locations missing their fetch and shelf parameters are skipped and logged by name**, not approximated. Zero wind setup leaves a pressure-only curve that is smooth, plausible and wrong — and still clears the significance gate. No Java write path sets those columns, so every admin-created coastal location lands there.
- **The chart and the "N m above normal" chip cannot contradict each other.** Agreement is guaranteed by construction (one input field, one pure function, one set of coastal parameters); a whole-day **envelope** check catches the one case construction cannot, a stale carrier. Deliberately not an equality test at the high-water hour: `Readings` records no sample timestamp, so a guessed hour would suppress good charts on most days — a guard that fires on correct data is worse than no guard.
- Lite sees the chart blurred rather than hidden, matching the tide chart and fact chips.

### Changed — the masthead joined the system the rest of the app already speaks

- **The wordmark belonged to nothing.** `/logo.png` beside IBM Plex Sans `text-4xl font-extrabold` was generic in a way no other surface still is — every panel below it had moved to the Kodachrome Field Guide vocabulary while the brand line stayed behind. The new `BrandLockup` is three stacked lines behind a film-perforation spine: a mono coral kicker (`Field guide to light`), a Newsreader 600 wordmark, and the tagline in serif italic. Brand presence now comes from texture rather than a badge, which is why there is **no image in the lockup at all**. The bitmap keeps the roles where a bitmap is the only option — favicon, apple-touch, PWA icons — but under their own filenames, so `frontend/public/logo.png` itself is now referenced by nothing in the app. It is left in place rather than deleted: `favicon.png` is a byte-identical copy of it, and working out which of the two is the original is a separate job from a rebrand.
- **The tagline described the inputs, not the payoff.** "AI sunrise, sunset, and aurora forecasting" listed what the pipeline consumes; **`Golden hour, forecast and ranked by AI`** says what the reader gets. Swept everywhere it is used *as a tagline* — the header, all three auth screens, the app `<title>`, the **PWA manifest `name`** (the install card is a tagline slot, and it was the copy most likely to be missed), and the landing site's five mastheads and homepage title. Descriptive prose that merely mentions sunrise/sunset/aurora forecasting — the Terms' legal description of the service, the landing feature copy — is untouched, because it is a sentence and not a strapline.
- **One coral, and only here.** The handoff spec said the kicker uses `--color-plex-gold`, believing that token to be `#E8593F`; in this codebase it is bone `#F2E7D3`, byte-identical to `--color-plex-text`, so following the spec literally would have rendered the kicker in exactly the wordmark's colour and flattened the three lines into one tone. The reference build had coral as its own token all along. **New `--color-plex-coral`**, deliberately *not* `--color-verdict-standdown` (`#C8452F`), which it sits near in hue: the two must never be confusable, so it is warmer and lighter and confined to the lockup. This does not reopen the "chrome is bone, never colour" rule — that rule exists so nothing competes with a verdict, and the masthead is the one surface carrying no forecast to compete with. ~5.2:1 on `--plex-bg`, above AA for the 10px it is set at.
- **Newsreader 600 is now bundled** (`fonts.js`, +24 KB woff2). It was declared in `@theme` but only 400/500 were self-hosted, so a `font-semibold` wordmark would have been *synthesised* from 500 — the failure mode is not a missing font but a silently smeared one at 40px.
- **The auth screens are left-aligned now**, dropping the `text-center` they carried. Not a preference: the spine is the whole device, and centred it reads as a stray rule rather than a film edge.
- The wordmark stays the page `<h1>` and carries the entire accessible name — the kicker and tagline are plain paragraphs, the spine is `aria-hidden`. `BrandLockup.test.jsx` pins that, plus the single-`PhotoCast`-node invariant that `LoginPage`'s `getByText` quietly depended on, because the e2e that would otherwise catch a demoted heading does not run in CI.

### Changed — the "Close to home" filters are remembered, and now say what they cut

- **The narrowing survives a reload.** "Under 30 minutes, 4★ and up" describes the reader's own tolerance for a drive, not a per-visit whim — it does not change between page loads, yet the block charged for it again on every one. All three chips now persist to localStorage through the existing `useLocalStorageState` hook. Not cleared on logout, matching the map's `mapFilterMinStars`: these are display preferences, and the postcode they are measured from lives server-side behind auth.
- **A remembered filter cannot become an unexplained empty block** — the chips carry their own on-state and `Clear` appears beside them the moment anything is active, so the cause is always on screen next to the effect.
- **A stored step is honoured only while it is still a step.** Storage outlives the code that wrote it, and `nextStep` returns "Any" for a value it does not recognise — so dropping 45 from `DRIVE_STEPS` would have left a returning user's chip reading "Any drive time" while it quietly hid half the set. `storedStep` sanitises on read; the value is inert until the next tap overwrites it.
- **The toggle is set by value, not by updater.** The persisting setter has no functional-update form: `setTideOnly(on => !on)` flips React state while `JSON.stringify`-ing the updater to `undefined`, so the chip would come back **on** after a reload the user had just turned it off in. A test pins the off case specifically.
- **Each chip now names its dimension in both states.** "Any drive" read as a mood rather than a control and gave no hint that tapping cycles through ceilings — it is `Any drive time` / `≤ 30 min drive`. "Tide only" named the subject but not the test and was being read as "coastal spots"; it is now **`Right tide only`**, which is what `CloseToHomeService.tideLabel` actually encodes: a king or spring tide, or the tide state that location is at its best in — never simply that it is on the coast. An InfoTip beside `Narrow` carries the full explanation, because a `title` is unreachable on touch and this block is read on a phone.

### Changed — the hover preview sets Claude's sentence in Claude's voice

- Serif italic is this app's typographic mark for **generated prose** — the drill-down gloss, the map overlay summary, the InfoTip card body and the tide-run phrase all carry it. The "Close to home" hover preview was the one surface rendering the same sentence in the UI sans, so a summary changed voice depending on whether you hovered a card or opened the drill-down. Geometry matches InfoTip's card body, the other serif block that floats over the page.

### Fixed — the tide run was anchored to a cove nobody could place

- **Every spring and king tide chart was drawn for Breil Nook, a small cove on Flamborough Head.** Not a bad roll — a structural one. `selectRepresentative` picked the biggest single-day range anywhere in the run, and tidal range on this coast **grows southward** toward the Humber approaches, so across a 53-location roster running Bamburgh to Bridlington the maximum reliably lands at the southern end. The card named it in the footer as the honest caveat, but naming a place the reader cannot locate carries no information: it makes the range, the curve and the alignment verdict read as arbitrary rather than as measurements of somewhere.
- **New `photocast.tide-run.preferred-anchor`** names the location the run is drawn for. Range picks the most dramatic version of the event; the anchor picks the most legible one, and legibility is what naming it in the footer was for. Blank restores pure biggest-range selection, so the behaviour is opt-in.
- **The anchor must be drawable, not merely present.** Matching on name alone would return a representative whose every day is then dropped for want of a high or a low — emptying the run and silently deleting a topic another location could have carried. A run that does not reach the anchor (a Yorkshire-only topic) falls back to biggest range rather than claiming a coastline it never covered.
- Matched by **name**, case-insensitively and trimmed: ids differ between local H2 and production, so a configured id would be a foot-gun that silently selects the wrong coast. The trade — renaming a location in the Admin UI quietly disables the preference — degrades to the previous behaviour rather than breaking.

### Fixed — "Close to home" stopped halfway through the horizon it advertises

- **The block reads "next 3 days" and then stopped at day 3's sunrise.** `MAX_WINDOWS` was a flat 3 while `HORIZON_DAYS` is 3 *days*, so on a Friday the three soonest windows ran out at Sunday sunrise and **Sunday sunset was dropped** — inside the stated horizon, present in the candidate set, and plainly visible in the Plan grid directly below, which builds up to 6 events. Nothing on the panel said a window had been withheld.
- **The cap is now derived from the horizon** (`HORIZON_DAYS × EVENTS_PER_DAY`) rather than written as a number beside it, so the two cannot drift apart again. That makes it non-binding today, which is the point: `collectNearby` already stops at 3 distinct dates, so the horizon bounds the block and the cap survives as a guard on the grouping, not as a product decision about how far it reaches. A count the user can read but not act on is the failure `withinReach` was fixed for; a horizon they can read but not see is the same failure one level up.
- `windowsCappedAtThree` pinned exactly the broken behaviour, so it is replaced by two tests: one building the full 3 days × 2 events and asserting the **last day's sunset** survives (highest rating in the fixture, so a truncation cannot pass by discarding something dull), and one pinning `MAX_WINDOWS >= HORIZON_DAYS × 2` as arithmetic so tightening either constant alone fails.
### Added — a build-depth design for the all-in architecture, and the six false doc claims found writing it

- **`docs/engineering/all-in-build-plan.md`** is the resolved, re-verified position; `all-in-architecture-design.md` holds the reasoning and now carries corrections where the two disagreed. The raw working drafts sit under `docs/engineering/all-in/`, each headed with what supersedes it. **No production code changes** — this is design only.
- **The shape of the change.** Three concerns are tangled and every symptom is downstream of the tangle: selection reads publication (`ForecastTaskCollector` builds its roster from `briefingService.getCachedBriefing()`, which the cycle tail rewrites — this is the T+3 defect's mechanism); persistence is split by *engine* rather than by *kind of fact* (batch results to `cached_evaluation`, only triage rows to `forecast_evaluation`, so the ERA5 harness's population is by construction the set of slots that were thrown away); and eleven gates key on classifications guarding ~£193/year combined. Target: `SLATE → SELECT → EVALUATE → RECORD → PROJECT → PUBLISH`, each stage reading only upstream, with one append-only spine.
- **One rule decides what survives: classify for display, never gate on the classification.** Nearly every gate has a twin doing honest work elsewhere — the stability *classifier* feeds the Plan tab and the prompt while its *snapshot and gates* go; `BriefingVerdictEvaluator` keeps GO/MARGINAL/STANDDOWN while `BriefingGatingPolicy`'s use of a verdict to skip a Claude call goes; the triage *reason* still colours unscored cells via `EvaluationViewService`. Getting this boundary wrong is the one way the work causes visible damage, and adversarial review caught three separate attempts to delete a display path along with its gate — one inherited from the design document itself.
- **What the gates actually hide.** Seven of eleven are bookkeeping and conceal no sky condition. Of the four that do: solar low cloud >80% is already handled harder by the prompt (`>60% = BLOCKED`, a rating 1–2 ceiling that beats any canvas); a misaligned tide is scored **1** deterministically by `TideVisitor` and averaged in by `RatingCombiner`, never a prompt lever since v2.13.2; and **the visibility gate is inverted** — triage discards below 5,000 m while `PromptBuilder.java:163-169` scores 2–8 km valley mist *up* as potential inversion, so the gate has been binning the mornings `InversionScoreCalculator` exists to find and recording them at a canned rating of 1. Only **precipitation** is a real gap: Claude receives the millimetres but has no rule to act on them, so a numeric rule must land in `SYSTEM_PROMPT` before that gate comes out.
- **Two hazards recorded before anyone runs the drops.** `cloud_verification` holds `ON DELETE CASCADE` onto `forecast_evaluation` (`V129:43`) — dropping that table destroys all 25,730 ERA5 rows, the only non-self-referential evidence outside the empty `actual_outcome`. And `pipeline_phase` is `@Enumerated(EnumType.STRING)`, so renaming its values without a data migration makes every historical row throw on read.
- **The calibration gate reports a measured zero where it has no data.** `CalibrationBucket.java:58-61` returns `null` for every rate — correctly — but `missedOpportunities` and `wastedTrips` are declared `int` and come back as literal `0`. The first of those is the count designated to gate any future relaxation of the cloud-approach veto, so the one number authorised to unblock a threshold change currently reads "zero missed opportunities" from zero observations.
- **Six doc claims read plausibly and were false**, continuing the pattern of v2.16.x. Two are in `CLAUDE.md` and are **not yet fixed**: it states the project runs on H2 locally (it does not — Postgres on every runtime profile; `h2` is `<scope>test</scope>` at `pom.xml:250-254`, and `application-local.yml` has Flyway *disabled*), and it cites `src/test/java/**/regression/` as the protected test path (that directory does not exist; prompt-regression is selected by JUnit tag via `pom.xml:23,29-32`). A third was self-inflicted: the design was first written against a branch that is not an ancestor of `main`, shifting every `ForecastTaskCollector` citation past line 534 by +16.

## [v2.17.7] - 2026-07-31

### Fixed — the release script treated "ahead of origin" as nothing to worry about

- **`release.sh` cut the CHANGELOG promotion branch from local main without checking whether local main had anything origin did not**, so an unpushed commit rode into the promotion PR and came back squashed under its message. That is how a 497-line design doc ended up inside `docs: promote [Unreleased] to v2.17.6 (#380)`, and why the script then aborted at its own sync step with `Not possible to fast-forward` — the squash had made the commit it was standing on unreachable. The guard read `LOCAL != REMOTE` and reached for `git pull --ff-only`, but "differs" is three states and only *behind* is the one that fast-forwards: on an **ahead** branch `--ff-only` prints `Already up to date.` and exits 0, so the check passed by saying nothing was wrong. Ahead and diverged now abort, listing the offending commits and how to clear them; behind still fast-forwards.

### Fixed — a four-location region could declare itself "Worth it" on one location, and read HIGH confidence doing it

- **`rollUpVerdict` is a ratio with no minimum n.** GO fires when `goCount` reaches 20% of the *viable* (non-STANDDOWN) slots, and `1 * 100 >= viable * 20` holds for every viable count up to 5 — so a single GO location carries the entire region whenever five or fewer of its locations are viable. Measured across the live roster, the stand-downs needed to reach that band were: **Teesdale 0** (unconditional, 4 voting locations), **Tyne and Wear 2** of 7, North York Moors 4 of 9, then 19 / 50 / 53 / 65 for the four large regions. Two stand-downs out of seven is an ordinary British morning, so the small regions were reaching "Worth it" off one location on exactly the mixed days the verdict exists to adjudicate.
- **`ConfidenceDeriver` did not compensate — it inverted.** The `ratingRange >= 2` downgrade is a min–max statistic, so it saturates with n: near-certain to fire across 70 locations, uncommon across 4. The channel meant to read "trust this less" was in practice reading roster size, backwards. A `voting <= 5` roster is now **floored at LOW** regardless of horizon, and `voting < 10` downgrades one band — both thresholds derived from the rollup arithmetic rather than picked, since falling into the degenerate band takes `voting - 5` stand-downs, which is under half the roster exactly when `voting < 10`.
- **The floor reads the roster the *verdict* uses, not the one coverage uses.** `BriefingHierarchyBuilder` lets only non-canopy slots vote; coverage counts every slot that could carry a rating. For a wood-bearing region in bluebell season these genuinely differ — a scored wood counts toward coverage but still never votes — so `RegionRoster` carries both as a record rather than two transposable ints.
- **V137 merges the small regions** (Teesdale → Yorkshire Dales, Tyne and Wear → Northumberland, North York Moors → North Yorkshire Coast), leaving four regions at 77 / 59 / 58 / 33 voting locations. The 20% ratio was never wrong; the regions were never large enough for a ratio to mean anything.
- **What this does not fix, stated plainly.** Degeneracy is keyed on the *viable* count — `goCount + marginalCount`, computed per day and per event from runtime verdicts — not on the roster. A 77-location region whose 73 worst locations stand down has 4 viable, and one GO among them still carries it; regional-scale overcast in the North East is not exotic. So the merge **raises the bar rather than removing the failure**, and the new confidence floor, which keys on roster size, cannot fire in that case. Separately, the 77/59/58/33 figures count *locations*, while slots are built per `(date, event)` and skipped on `!supportsTargetType` — and V138 adds nine SUNRISE-only locations, so per-event rosters are smaller than these numbers and were never measured. `rollUpVerdict` is deliberately untouched: a minimum-n term there is a product decision about the star signal, not a data cleanup.
- **Two locations were also simply misfiled.** Stanley Ghyll Force is a Lakeland ravine at Boot in Eskdale — two miles from Muncaster Castle Gardens, already correctly in the Lake District, and ninety from High Force. Hardwick Hall Country Park is at Sedgefield, not in Teesdale.
- Emptied regions are **disabled, not deleted**: `model_test_result.region_id` is a NOT NULL FK recording which region a historical A/B/C run covered. Both briefing caches key on region *name*, so `daily_briefing_cache` is cleared (its JSON carries names inline and would serve a phantom "Teesdale") and the affected `cached_evaluation` rows are deleted rather than rewritten — `cache_key` is UNIQUE, so rewriting `Teesdale|…` to `The Yorkshire Dales|…` would collide with the row already there.

### Added — the Durham Heritage Coast enters the roster (V138)

- The roster ran down the coast to Marsden, Trow and Souter, then stopped until Redcar — roughly **thirty miles of unrepresented east-facing shoreline** through Sunderland, Seaham, Easington, Blackhall and Hartlepool, none of which the app could brief on. Ten locations added, Roker Pier to Hartlepool Headland.
- ⚠️ **Coordinates are unverified estimates** of the shooting position, not surveyed values; `docs/engineering/durham-heritage-coast-candidates.geojson` ships alongside for checking them on a map. A wrong coordinate fails silently by briefing the wrong grid cell.
- **Tide and solar preferences are sourced from published location guides**, not inferred from site type. Chemical Beach and Shippersea Bay are **LOW only** — the chaldron wheels surface only at low water (one guide quotes a 1.2 m tide height) and Shippersea is reached around Beacon Point on a falling tide, so that one is an access constraint rather than a preference. Seaham North Pier moved from HIGH to LOW/MID because the compositional element in every published frame is the Featherbed Rocks platform, which needs the tide off it. Hartlepool Headland gained **SUNSET** as the one location here with a genuine westward aspect — it is surrounded by sea on three sides — and is the only site judged to earn the doubled evaluation cost.
- Storm-surge parameters interpolate the V60 neighbours (bearing 90–110, fetch 240–250 km, shelf 27–28 m) rather than Redcar's, whose 400 km / 30 m reflects it facing north into Tees Bay. `elevation_m` is left NULL rather than guessed, since `InversionScoreCalculator` reads it.
- This does **not** make a standalone County Durham region viable and the migration records why: nine of the ten are County Durham, which with Wharton Park gives a voting roster of 10 — better than Teesdale's four, still inside the failure mode above.

### Fixed — the scroll bar under each "Close to home" filmstrip was a picture of a scroll bar

- **It looked exactly like a scrollbar and answered nothing.** The gold rail under each window's card strip was built as a read-only position indicator: thumb width for the visible fraction, offset for the scrolled fraction, and no pointer handlers at all. The strip hides the native bar (`scrollbar-width: none`), so for a mouse user that rail *was* the only scrollbar on screen — grabbing it did nothing, and with four cards of a twenty-card window visible there was no visible handle to the other sixteen. Click now jumps and drag now tracks, both centred on the pointer and clamped at both ends. Kept `aria-hidden` and out of the tab order deliberately: it duplicates scrolling keyboard users already get by tabbing the cards, and a custom scrollbar in the tab order is one more stop before the next window.
- Scroll snapping is suspended for the duration of a drag. `scroll-snap-type: x mandatory` re-snaps every programmatic `scrollLeft` write, which turns a smooth drag into a card-by-card stutter that reads as the rail fighting back; restoring it on release re-snaps once, so the strip still lands on a card edge.
- A 2px bar is a fine thing to look at and an impossible one to hit, so the rail carries an invisible 16px hit target and thickens on hover — the bar itself stays hairline-quiet.
- **The thumb was also lying about how much was off-screen.** It re-measured on window `resize` and on scroll only, so a first measure taken before layout settled — or the panel resizing, or a font swapping, none of which fire `resize` — left a thumb sized for a strip that no longer existed. Reproduced in a browser at 16% on a strip that was showing 41%, self-correcting only once something happened to scroll it. The strip is now watched with a `ResizeObserver`. That inaccuracy was cosmetic while the rail was decorative; on a draggable rail it is a wrong pointer→offset mapping.

## [v2.17.6] - 2026-07-31

### Changed — "Next local window" is a place you can go, not a caption

- **Users read that block as *my next closest good opportunity* and act on it — it is the most-used entry point in "Close to home" — but it showed a name, a bare `· 4.0★` and a drive time, and withheld the three facts that actually decide the trip:** where the spot is, how far, and what the water is doing. It now carries what a card carries, in the card's order: kicker → name + the **same** star pill the cards use (`RatingPill`, extracted so a 4★ cannot read one way in the grid and another beside it) → region → `Today sunset 21:11 · 🚗 10 min · 4 mi · 🌊 spring tide` → `◍ Open on map →`. Tide is omitted when there is none; distance never is. Behaviour is unchanged — the same `onShowOnMap(date, targetType, locationName)` handler, the same hover preview, no second code path.
- **It now points at a location instead of describing one.** `CloseToHomeResponse.NextWindow` carried copies of the name, rating and drive time, which was survivable while it rendered as a label; showing region, distance and tide as well would have made six copied fields able to contradict the same location's card on the same screen. The record carries the `Card` itself, and `CloseToHomeService.toCard` is now the single place a card is built — the shortcut is by construction rank 1 of the first window, so a test pins the two equal field for field. `lead` stays false on the shortcut: the gold accent marks rank 1 of the first window *in the grid*, and spending it twice would mean nothing.
- The shortcut gets a real tap target — hover/focus background, rounded on three corners, the gold left rule kept because that is what holds it inside the breadcrumb rather than floating it out as a card. The padding is bought back with a negative margin so promoting a label to a card did not shove the breadcrumb's sentence around. It stays a native `<button>` rather than `role="button"` + `tabindex="0"` + key handlers: Enter and Space already work, with no JavaScript.

### Fixed — three review findings: one contradiction, one dropped caveat, one stranded panel

- **The "Worth it" paragraph could name a different location than the lead card directly beneath it.** "Close to home" ranks candidates in three places, each producing a gold element of the same block for the same event — the lead card, "Next local window", and the breadcrumb's prose. Two of them broke a rating tie on the shorter drive; the third kept a bare `Stream.max` on rating, which resolves ties by iteration order. So a 4★ tie between a 10-minute drive and a 45-minute one put Copt Hill on the card, Copt Hill in the shortcut, and Angel of the North in the sentence above them — one block naming two places for one sunrise. The rule is now a single `byRatingThenDrive` comparator all three call, because three copies of an ordering is three chances for one to drift, and one had. The tie test asserts all three selections rather than the pair that broke last.
- **Mobile hid the one thing that says which coastline a tide run was drawn for.** The `≤767px` rule dropped `.tr-loc` along with the chart legend, and `.tr-loc` is the only place the representative location is named. That left clock times on a phone with nothing saying which shore they belong to — losing the ~20-minute caveat precisely where the times are most likely to be read in the field. The legend still goes; the location stays. The existing test could not catch it: `getByTestId` asserts DOM presence and jsdom does not evaluate media queries.
- **The hover preview could be left floating over the map overlay.** Clicking a card swaps a full-viewport overlay in under a pointer that has not moved, covering the card without reliably firing `mouseleave` — the same "the DOM moved out from under a `position: fixed` preview" case the filmstrip's `onScroll` already guarded, and the preview sits above the overlay. All three surfaces that open the map now dismiss on the way out. Cosmetic only: `pointer-events: none` meant the map underneath was always fully interactive.

### Fixed — two counts on the Plan tab that described sets nobody was looking at

- **The block header counted the busiest single window, so a location in only one window vanished from the total.** Two windows holding one different spot each read "1 within reach" under a heading naming a radius. Summing them — the bug the busiest-window rule was itself a fix for — double-counts the other way, since a spot qualifying for three windows is still one place you can drive to. It is now the count of **unique locations** across windows, which is the only reading of "within reach" that matches the heading beside it.
- **The ordering note was printed once under the first window whatever its size, so a 3-card first window swallowed it for a 20-card second one** — the strip that ran off the edge was left looking like an arbitrary sample. It is now stated under **each** overflowing window, after that window's scroll rail, only when there is something off-screen (`> 4` cards) and never while the window is expanded into the ranked list, where nothing is hidden and "scroll for the rest" would be a lie. The clause naming the number stays gated on the same 900px media query as the 4.5-across sizing it describes.

### Fixed — the rollback path could exit 1 in silence, and the pre-release manifest stopped recording the schema

- **`rollback.sh` died with no output on a manifest missing any key it reads.** `field()` is `grep | head | cut`; under `set -euo pipefail` a missing key makes grep exit 1, pipefail promotes it to the pipeline's status, the bare command substitution makes it the *assignment's* status, and `set -e` kills the run — before printing anything. Every explicit error below it was therefore unreachable for the one input it was written for, including `"manifest is missing image digests — cannot roll back safely"`. Reproduced, then fixed with `|| true`. This is the only defect here that would have been discovered mid-incident, in the script the repo calls PLAN B.
- **`pre-release-backup.sh` has recorded `schema_version: "unknown"` since #364,** which deleted the `SCHEMA_VERSION_SQL` assignment and left the use. The unbound variable was swallowed by the trailing `|| echo 'unknown'`, so no release failed and nothing looked wrong. Restored, with a note on why the constant is duplicated in both scripts rather than sourced: `deploy.yml` materialises each script *alone* out of the release tag, so a `source` of a sibling path resolves to nothing on the host.
- **The new guard validates the RESULT, not the definition.** An unbound query variable is only one route to `"unknown"` — psql failing (database mid-restart, wrong credentials) produces the same value through the same `||`, with the error hidden by `2>/dev/null`. Deliberately a warning rather than an abort: the script reserves aborting for the dump and the digests, which are what a rollback actually needs.
- **CI now runs ShellCheck, and the flag is the whole point.** `SC2154` is off by default for uppercase names, so plain `shellcheck scripts/*.sh` exits **0** on the broken file and would have caught nothing; `-o check-unassigned-uppercase` exits 1 and names line 137. Verified both ways against the pre-fix version. Nothing in the repo executes `scripts/*.sh`, so this is the only gate they have. Step-level `if:` guards matching every other job, because a skipped required check blocks a merge rather than passing it.
- **Historical manifests are deliberately NOT being hand-repaired.** `schema_version` is advisory — nothing branches on it — but the *presence* of the key was load-bearing until the fix above, so an edit that dropped or mistyped it would have converted a cosmetic wrong number into a rollback that exits silently. The two fields that make a rollback work, the image digests and the dump path, have been correct throughout.

### Changed — the three `| grep -q` container probes, for the reason that is actually real

- **The SIGPIPE concern was measured and does not exist here.** 6,000 runs of the exact pattern under `set -euo pipefail` with the real 5-byte writer produced **zero** spurious failures; the same harness caught the failure 50/50 with a large writer, proving it can detect the defect. `docker inspect --format='{{.State.Running}}'` emits 5 bytes into a 64 KB pipe buffer and never writes again, so it cannot receive SIGPIPE. Recorded because the earlier characterisation of these three sites as latent SIGPIPE bugs was wrong.
- **They were changed for a different, real reason: `grep -q true` is an unanchored substring match.** It accepts `"untrue"`, and would accept the struct dump you get if the format string were ever shortened to `{{.State}}` — which contains the word `true` even for a stopped container, i.e. it fails **open**. Replaced with an exact comparison against a captured value, which fails closed. Verified identical in the running / stopped / container-missing / docker-absent cases, and opposite on malformed output.
- `pre-release-backup.sh` also gains the `command -v docker` precondition the other two scripts already had. Without it a missing binary still failed closed, but reported *"goldenhour-db is not running here"* — which sends you looking at the container on a host where the problem is the host.

### Changed — the forecast list returns 2 past days instead of 7, and the SWR cache collects its orphans

- **`GET /api/forecast` now returns T-2..T+5.** The payload is cached client-side for instant paint and the past half was the larger half: past days are fully dense — every one was scored when it was T+0, and WILDLIFE/WATERFALL locations carry ~16 HOURLY rows each per day — while T+4/T+5 are never batch-evaluated. This reverts part of f58621f0, which added the 7-day window and the DateStrip's dimmed past chips together; the strip keeps two chips instead of seven.
- **Not zero, and the reason is a timezone bug it would have exposed.** `computeAutoSelection` picks the initial date from the browser's *local* date while the window and the strip's "today" are both UTC, so west of UTC auto-selection legitimately asks for T-1. At T-0 that date is absent and the selection silently degrades to a fallback.
- **`BriefingEvaluationController` had the same `minusDays(7)` and now shares the constant.** The Map's date strip derives its dates from the forecast payload alone, so any score returned for an older date had no chip that could ever surface it.
- **SWR cache: one entry per namespace, at the current key generation, swept at write time.** Keys are `namespace:role`, giving 8 reachable keys per generation, and nothing collected the 6 a single user cannot be using — read-triggered eviction can't reach them, because collection requires reading that exact key. Deliberately **not** evict-the-other-namespace-and-retry: the two payloads genuinely don't fit under iOS's ceiling, so that would turn today's stable outcome into alternating thrash where each write evicts the other and the next load starts cold.
- **This does not make both caches coexist, and should not be read as doing so.** ~2.6 MB plus a larger forecasts payload still loses against ~5 MB on iOS. What it removes is the orphans that made the loss permanent and unexplainable. The ~4 MB forecasts figure driving all of this is still an unmeasured code comment.
- **A cache-generation guard closes a cross-account leak.** `logout` awaits a network round-trip, sweeps, and clears the token *after* that — so there is a window where the sweep has run and the tree is still mounted. A revalidation resolving inside it re-planted the previous account's full payload under the previous account's role key, right after the cache was supposedly cleared. Writers now capture the generation before fetching and the write is refused if it changed. An unmount-cancellation flag does not close that window.

## [v2.17.5] - 2026-07-31

### Added — a spring tide is a RUN, and its days now say when the water actually lands

- **Three problems, one shape.** A multi-day spring tide produced several pill cards whose day word only appeared when a tide happened to align with a solar event — `HotTopicEventEnricher.resolveTideEvent` returns null on zero alignment, and `maskExpired` drops an alignment once its event has passed, so most days of a run carried no day at all; the tide times (`LW 22:19`) and the solar times lived in different parts of the UI, so *does low water land near sunrise?* — the only question a tidal photographer is asking — was left as mental arithmetic; and nothing said the cards belonged to one continuous event.
- **The ordering spine stays time.** One card per day, in date order, unchanged. A single multi-row card for a four-day event sits at one point in a chronological list and breaks the flow for every topic around it — tried, reverted. Ranking is by **emphasis**: only the aligned day's verdict takes the topic accent. Demoting or collapsing the badly-timed days was rejected for introducing a second, competing ordering into one list, and for hiding that the run *continues* on the days someone planning Thursday needs.
- **New `TideRunDay` + `TideRunBuilder`** derive, per day: the range against the location's own mean, every extreme in the local day, that day's sunrise and sunset, the sea state, and a plain-language verdict (`LW 05:44 · 34m after sunrise`). `TideRunRow.jsx` draws a 24-hour chart — cosine interpolation between extrema, extended half a cycle past both ends so the curve fills the axis instead of flat-lining, night shaded, solar events as amber rules — and the pill carries a `SPRING RUN 2/4` chip.
- **The chart replaces the fact chips rather than joining them.** The chips already carried range, anomaly, HW/LW and seas; printing both would state the same four numbers twice, once as arithmetic the reader has to do.
- **One location for the whole run, and it is named.** The representative is chosen once — the biggest single-day range anywhere in the run — not per day, or the curve could jump between coastlines mid-run and a reader comparing Tuesday to Thursday would be comparing two places. Alignment genuinely differs by ~20 minutes across a coastline a topic may span, so the footer says *at Seaham*: naming it is the honest form of the caveat.
- **Days that cannot be drawn are dropped, not faked.** A day needs both a high and a low at that location to have a range or a curve; without them the pill falls back to its fact chips. A **one-day run claims no peak** — every day is trivially the biggest, and a "peak range" badge on a lone card asserts a comparison that was never made.
- The chart is `aria-hidden`; the verdict string carries the same meaning in words and is pinned by a test, because the chart is decorative to a screen reader and the verdict is the entire accessible answer.

### Fixed — "Close to home" stated a count it gave no way to act on

- **The window showed four cards under a header reading "20 within reach".** `MAX_CARDS_PER_WINDOW` silently discarded the rest, so the number was readable and unreachable, with nothing saying why those four. The backend cap is **gone**: every qualifier is carried, and `withinReach` can no longer disagree with the list — a test pins the invariant.
- **Three complementary changes, because a filmstrip alone hides rank and count.** Cards became a scroll-snap filmstrip sized to 4.5 across, so the half card *is* the affordance; the count became a button that expands the window into a ranked list (the strip makes everything reachable, the list makes the ranking legible once it scrolls past the fold); and block-head filters (max drive / min rating / tide only) cut the set so overflow is rarely reached. Filters apply **before anything is counted**, so no number on the block can describe a set the user is not looking at.
- **Thresholds follow the data, not the mockup.** The spec asked for 3.0★/3.5★/4.0★ — ratings are whole stars, so a 3.5 threshold could only ever behave as 4. Steps are Any → 3 → 4 → 5, and every cycling chip returns to Any rather than stranding on.
- A window that filters to nothing **keeps its head** and says so, so the day/event rhythm does not shuffle as the set narrows. Within a window, equal ratings now break on the **shorter drive** rather than the alphabet — distance still never outranks a better-rated spot further out.

### Added — hover preview on the nearby cards, and the next local window opens the map

- **"Next local window" was the one dead end on a panel where every other location is one tap from the pin.** It names a location, a date and an event — the same three facts a card carries — so it is now a button calling the same handler, with the same lift, focus ring and `◍ Open on map →` affordance.
- **Cards and the next-window button preview on hover**: name, stars, drive, Claude's sentence, and the two score bars. Sourced *only* from data the Plan tab already holds — the briefing score index and the map's own forecast rows — so sweeping a pointer across a grid never becomes a burst of requests. Knowing nothing about a slot shows nothing; a hover shortcut is not worth a fetch.
- **The rating deliberately comes from the card, not the score index.** The scores and summary come from the index because the panel does not carry them, but a preview that disagreed with the card it hangs off is the exact failure `CloseToHomeService`'s single-source contract exists to prevent.
- Pointer-only and viewport-gated — hover has no touch equivalent, and on a phone a panel pinned beside the card would cover the grid it came from. Focus opens it too, so the shortcut is not mouse-only. `jsdom` has no `matchMedia`, so the shared test setup now polyfills it alongside `ResizeObserver`.

### Fixed — eight defects an adversarial review found in the three changes above

Four independent reviewers over the working diff, each with a different lens. Two findings were reached by two reviewers each, which is what marked them as real rather than plausible:

- **The tide chart's "Open on map" went nowhere useful.** It passed a handoff *object* — but tide topics carry `filterAction: null` and have no single region, so it matched neither of `App.handleShowOnMap`'s object branches and fell through to `kind: 'event'` with the object itself where a date string belongs: the overlay opened fitted to every pin, titled "Invalid Date". Now positional `(date, eventType, locationName)`, focused on the one location the curve was actually drawn for.
- **The filmstrip rendered slivers for short windows.** The `max-width` cap kept from the old grid compounded with the new percentage `flex-basis`, because the card's `100%` resolves against the *capped* container: a one-card window capped at 25% produced a ~56px card whose name wrapped over six lines, where the old grid gave 250px. The cap is gone — `flex: 0 0` already forbids growing, which is the only thing the cap was for.
- **The hover preview handed Lite users the two Pro scores.** The map popup gates both score blocks on `role !== 'LITE_USER'` and offers "Upgrade to Pro for Fiery Sky & Golden Hour scores" in their place; the values reach a Lite client regardless, so that gate is the UX layer and has to hold at *every* surface that renders them. The preview now shows the same upsell.
- **Two gold elements could name two different locations for one event.** The drive-time tiebreak went into `toWindow` but not `buildBreadcrumb` — and the earliest window is also `windows[0]`, so on a rating tie the "Next local window" and the lead card disagreed. Same comparator now.
- **The run chip counted down through the day.** `dayNumber`/`dayCount`/`peak` were derived from the topics that survived the solar-freshness filter rather than from the run, so `SPRING RUN 1/4` became `1/3` once Monday's events passed, and "peak range" could migrate onto a day that is not the biggest. The run is built from every day of the tide; a tide does not renumber itself because a sunset went by.
- **The king-tide pill lost the number that makes it king.** Swapping the chart in for the fact chips dropped absolute high water and its excess over the spring threshold, leaving only range-against-mean — a *spring* framing. King runs now lead with `high water 5.8 m · +0.4 m over spring`.
- **The strip clipped its own focus ring.** `overflow-x: auto` makes `overflow-y` compute to `auto`, so a card's 2px ring at 2px offset lost its top and bottom bars. Padding buys the 4px, a negative margin gives it back to the layout.
- Two smaller ones: `"0m after sunrise"` — a null statement dressed as a measurement, on the most emphatic day a run can have — now reads `at sunrise`; and `"Showing the top 4 of 10"` no longer appears at widths where four cards do not fit. The preview is also dismissed when the filmstrip scrolls, since it is `position: fixed` at coordinates captured on mouseenter and no `mouseleave` fires when the card slides out from under a stationary pointer.

### Fixed — a second audit pass, run before pushing, caught the first fix using the wrong breakpoint

- **"Showing the top 4 of 10" was suppressed at 639px, but four cards only fit from 900px.** The guard was `!isMobile`, and `useIsMobile` matches `(max-width: 639px)` — while the 4.5-across `flex-basis` lives inside `@media (min-width: 900px)`. Across the whole 640–899px band (768px iPad portrait included) one card filled the strip while the sentence still claimed four. The count in that sentence is a *fact about the CSS*, so the CSS is now the only thing that decides when it shows: the clause sits in a `.cth-wide-only` span gated on the same query as the sizing. A JS breakpoint mirroring a CSS one is a second source of truth, and this is what that costs.
- **The CHANGELOG entry above claimed the previous pills said nothing about which day they meant.** Not quite: `HotTopicEventEnricher.resolveTideEvent` gives tide topics a solar event whenever any location's tide aligns, and the pill's timing lead then renders "↓ Tomorrow sunset · 21:19". The day was missing only when alignment was zero or had been masked out as expired — common across a run, but not "none". Reworded to say what is true.
- `TideRunDay`'s javadoc example for `highWaterAnomaly` read `"+0.4 over spring"`; the only producer formats metres, so the field always carries `"+0.4 m over spring"`.

### Fixed — the cache-floor guard shipped in v2.17.4 with two wrong claims of its own

An adversarial review of #371 ran after it had already merged. It found two defects, both the same class the PR existed to fix: a confident number in a comment that nothing checks. The v2.17.4 entries above are corrected inline; the reasoning is here.

- **"16,318 characters" is not the length of anything.** The prompts measure **16,193** (inland) and **16,452** (coastal); their UTF-8 byte lengths are 16,365 and 16,626. The token half of that sentence is real — seven days of `api_call_log` genuinely put the cached prefix at ~4,322 tokens — so the attribution is now split: **tokens from production, characters measured from the tree**, because the log stores the count and not the string. The corrected ratio is ~3.75 chars/token and the derived floor ~15,350, not ~15,470.
- **`MIN_CACHEABLE_SYSTEM_PROMPT_CHARS` stays 15,500,** which sits above the corrected floor either way, so the guard errs strict and no assertion moved. The javadoc now says that margin is deliberate rather than incidental: the 4,322 reading never recorded *which* of the two prompts produced it, and their ratios differ (3.75 inland against 3.81 coastal), so the constant covers the spread instead of landing inside it.
- **"Bluebell and woodland caching is inert" is true only of the far-term path.** `ForecastTaskCollector` builds both with the same `decision.model()` as the sky path, so T+0/T+1 runs them on `BATCH_NEAR_TERM` — Sonnet by default (V92), floor **1,024 tokens**, a quarter of Haiku's. Woodland (~4,770 chars, order of 1,270 tokens) clears that and **does** cache; bluebell (~3,847) sits within measurement error of the line. The released entry also said those buckets could be "routed to Sonnet", which they already are.
- **Stated flatly, that invited someone to delete a live `cache_control` block as dead weight** — so the javadoc, the test's `@DisplayName` and both failure messages are Haiku-scoped now, and `BatchRequestFactory` says explicitly not to remove them. The assertion itself was always correct and is unchanged. **No Sonnet-derived character bound was added**: bluebell would sit inside 0.6% of it and flip on any wording tweak, which is a worse guard than none.
- **The "INTRADAY is reserved" claim #371 corrected in `CycleType` survived in two more places** — `PipelineRunSummary`'s `@param cycleType`, and `PipelineOrchestrator:266`, fifty lines below the call that registers `runIntradayCycle` against `intraday_forecast_refresh`.
- Mutation-verified with a **clean compile per run**, because the constant is a compile-time `static final int` and gets inlined into the test's bytecode, so an incremental build silently reuses a stale value and reports a pass. Floor 16,300 reddens the inland assertion **alone** — which is what demonstrates the coastal assertion is not tautological with it — 17,000 reddens both, 3,000 reddens the niche assertion.


### Changed — release.sh promotes the CHANGELOG itself, instead of printing instructions for it

- **The guard was right; making it a dead end was the mistake.** `release.sh` refused to tag while `[Unreleased]` held entries — correctly, since the alternative is 83 entries piling under one heading across 13 releases, which is what happened between v2.15.4 and v2.17.0. But it then printed a three-step recipe for a hand-made PR, so every release became two, and that PR's edit sat exactly where feature branches write their own entries. #371 conflicted on `CHANGELOG.md` for precisely this reason and had to be resolved by hand.
- **The stated reason it couldn't automate this was false.** The comment read *"it cannot commit the promotion itself, because main requires a PR"* — but `enforce_admins` is `false` on the branch protection, so an admin can push straight to `main`. The script now goes through a PR anyway, deliberately: a release tool whose normal operating mode is bypassing branch protection makes that protection decorative for every other change too. The bypass stays available and stays manual.
- **A planned `codeql.yml` change turned out to be unnecessary, which is the more useful finding.** CodeQL is a required check with no docs-only fast path, so a CHANGELOG-only PR looked like it would wait ~5 minutes on it. It doesn't: the required `CodeQL` context is published by the **github-advanced-security app**, not by the `CodeQL Analysis` workflow job, and it reports `neutral` in ~2s for a diff touching no analysable code. Measured on #372 — merged at 23:23:50Z while the job ran on until 23:25:25Z, 95 seconds *past* the merge. The workflow was never on the gate's critical path, and no workflow changed.
- **Four refusals, because a promotion that silently does the wrong thing is worse than one that stops.** It will not promote when the chosen target is not `main` HEAD (the commit would land on top, leaving the tag pointing at unpromoted notes — the exact outcome the guard exists to prevent); it aborts if the heading fails to insert; it aborts unless the rewrite adds **exactly 2 lines and removes 0**, so a rewrite that moved entry text can never reach a tag; and it refuses when `## [vX.Y.Z]` is **already present** at the target.
- **That fourth refusal exists because an adversarial review found the first three were all blind to the same case, and two independent reviewers converged on it.** Inserting a *second* identical version heading still adds exactly 2 lines and removes 0, still leaves `[Unreleased]` empty, and the pre-tag check is `grep -q`, which matches the first of the two. So a re-run would have tagged a CHANGELOG whose notes were split across two same-named sections carrying different dates — precisely the attribution corruption this whole section exists to prevent. **No race is needed: the script's own exit paths create the state.** The promotion PR merges, then the run stops before tagging — the user answers `n` at the final confirmation, or Ctrl-Cs the poll, or hits the timeout whose message says *"merge it, then re-run ./release.sh"*. `main` now carries the heading with no tag; later entries accumulate; the user re-runs at the same version (correctly — the tag is still free) and the duplicate lands. Reproduced against the real CHANGELOG before fixing, and the fix verified not to fire on a legitimate promotion or on near-miss versions (`v2.17.4` vs `v2.17.40`, `v12.17.4`).
- **The cleanup trap also caught `INT`/`TERM`, not just `EXIT`.** The longest window in the script is a 15-minute poll, which is exactly where a Ctrl-C lands, and that was the worst moment to leave someone on a promotion branch with a half-applied rewrite. It now also unstages and discards its own CHANGELOG edit before returning to `main` — safe because start-up already guarantees a clean tree, so anything modified there is the script's own regenerable rewrite — and removes its temp file. Separately, the script anchors to the repo root, since the promotion read `CHANGELOG.md` by relative path and would otherwise have died *after* creating the branch when run from a subdirectory.
- Verified by replaying it against the v2.17.4 promotion: the generated rewrite reproduces the hand-made #372 diff **byte-for-byte**. Edge cases covered — absent `[Unreleased]` heading, duplicate headings (inserts once), mutated entry text (line-count guard refuses), missing/unauthenticated `gh`, pre-existing promotion branch, PR closed unmerged, and a bounded 15-minute wait that leaves the branch pushed rather than hanging.

## [v2.17.4] - 2026-07-31

### Fixed — the forecast prompt clears Haiku's cache floor by 5%, and nothing recorded it

- **Anthropic's minimum cacheable prefix is model-dependent and not monotonic**: 1,024 tokens on Sonnet, **4,096 on Haiku 4.5**. Below it, a request carrying `cache_control` does not error or warn — it returns `cache_creation_input_tokens: 0` and pays the full input rate forever. `BATCH_FAR_TERM` is Haiku (V92), so every T+2/T+3 evaluation depends on clearing that number.
- **Token count from seven days of production `api_call_log`; character counts measured from the tree:** the cached prefix is **~4,322 tokens**, and the prompts are **16,193 characters** (inland) / **16,452** (coastal) — roughly 3.75 chars/token, putting the floor near ~15,350 characters. The live prompt clears it by roughly **5%**, and no comment, constant or test recorded that. *(Corrected after release — this entry originally read "~4,322 tokens for 16,318 characters … floor at ~15,470", and 16,318 is not the length of either prompt. See [Unreleased].)* A routine edit trimming a few paragraphs would have switched caching off across the whole far-term bucket with no failing test and no operational signal.
- `PromptBuilder.MIN_CACHEABLE_SYSTEM_PROMPT_CHARS` now carries the derivation, and `SystemPromptCacheabilityTest` pins the inland and coastal prompts against it. The bound is the *cacheability* boundary rather than today's length, so ordinary rewording stays free and only a real reduction fails. Mutation-verified: raising the floor to 17,000 reddens exactly those two assertions.
- **It is a proxy, not an oracle** — characters per token vary with content, so a markup-heavy rewrite could fall under 4,096 tokens while still clearing a character check. The constant says so, and says to re-measure with `messages.count_tokens` against `claude-haiku-4-5` instead.
- **Bluebell and woodland prompts are ~1,030 and ~1,270 tokens, so on the far-term (Haiku) path** the `cache_control` blocks `BatchRequestFactory` attaches to those two buckets are **inert**. Left that way deliberately: padding them past 4,096 tokens would cost more input than caching could recover. A test pins the state so it is visible rather than surprising. *(Corrected after release — this entry originally called the blocks inert unconditionally and said they could be "routed to Sonnet for its lower floor". Both buckets already run on Sonnet at T+0/T+1, where woodland does cache. See [Unreleased].)*

### Fixed — three stale doc claims that each sent an investigation the wrong way

- **`BatchRequestFactory` said "~3,600-token system prompt".** The real figure is ~4,320 — and 3,600 is *below* Haiku's 4,096 floor, so the comment argued the opposite of the truth about its own `cache_control` block.
- **`CycleType` said INTRADAY was "reserved … do not use yet" and that "no INTRADAY phases are wired today".** Both false since V105: `runIntradayCycle()` exists, is registered against `intraday_forecast_refresh`, is seeded ACTIVE, and records a `STABILITY_RECLASSIFY` phase. The javadoc had been wrong for as long as the cycle has been running.
- **CLAUDE.md described `AuroraStateCache` as a four-state FSM** (`IDLE → MONITORING → MODERATE/STRONG`). It is `private enum State { IDLE, ACTIVE }` — two states. QUIET/MINOR/MODERATE/STRONG is the alert *level*, a separate field, not a state.
- Recorded together because they share a failure mode: each was load-bearing for a decision, each read plausibly, and each was only caught by opening the file it described.

### Fixed — a 4★ popup with no score bars, and a footer denying the evaluation above it

- **#356 coalesced the *rating* across the two score sources and left the scores behind.** `popupRating` preferred `briefingScore`, while the score bars a few lines below still read `forecast.fierySkyPotential` — null on a triaged row, because Claude was never called. The block guards on `!= null`, so it rendered nothing rather than failing: four stars above an empty space.
- **The footer then contradicted the popup it sat in**, printing "not evaluated by Claude due to heavy low cloud at the solar horizon" beneath a rating and narrative. `buildGeneratedFooter` now takes `triageSuperseded` and drops that clause when a live evaluation outranks the triage verdict; the disclaimer is untouched when nothing supersedes it, and a test pins that direction. The summary had the same asymmetry (`detail?.summary` only) and now falls back to `briefingScore.summary`.
- **This change is only correct *because* of #368, and would have been actively harmful before it.** Under the old unconditional cache-wins merge, `briefingScore` was routinely the *stale, optimistic* row — the Angel of the North popup that prompted this was a 4★ cached rating up to 47.9 hours old sitting over a current row triaged on 87–99% low cloud. Dressing that stale rating with matching score bars and suppressing its disclaimer would have made the wrong answer more convincing, not less. #368 made the merge prefer whichever source ran last, so `briefingScore` is now the freshest verdict available — and once that is true, coalescing the scores from it is right for exactly the reason coalescing the rating is.
- **`MapView` had always done this correctly** — `briefingScore?.fierySkyPotential ?? forecast?.fierySkyPotential` when drawing the marker arcs. Marker and popup disagreed by one `??`.
- **Why the tests missed it, which is the more useful part.** #356 built exactly the right fixture — a `rating: null, triageReason: 'HEAVY_CLOUD'` forecast row against a live cached score — but put it in `MapViewStarFilter.test.jsx`, where neither half carries a `fierySkyPotential` because the marker tests only ask whether the pin survives the veto. Meanwhile all 150 `MarkerPopupContent` tests build on a `BASE_FORECAST` that *has* scores, so the guard was never once exercised against a null. #356 was mutation-verified, and that could not help: **mutation testing proves the assertions you wrote will bite, not that you wrote the assertion.**

### Fixed — a stale cached rating outranked the current forecast, and the map showed it as live

- **`EvaluationViewService.mergeToView` let the cached evaluation win unconditionally.** Since a triaged slot makes no Claude call and therefore writes no cached rating, the only way a rating coexists with a triage is that **the rating came from an earlier, more optimistic run**. The merge had no freshness comparison at all, despite already receiving both timestamps.
- **Measured in production**, four days across two neighbouring locations (Copt Hill and Angel of the North, 11.8 km apart): every 4★ cached rating was stale — one by **47.9 hours** — against a current row triaged `HIGH_CLOUD` on **87–99% low cloud at the solar horizon**. Every cached rating that *was* fresher than its forecast row scored **≤2**. All the optimism lived in superseded rows, and it was being served to the Plan grid and the map as the live verdict.
- **The merge now prefers whichever source ran last.** Ties go to the cache — a same-instant pair is one batch run writing both halves, and the cache carries strictly more (rating, prose, sub-scores). Unknown freshness also keeps the cache, which preserves the previous behaviour rather than inventing a new one; both `cached_evaluation` stamps are `nullable = false` and every in-memory writer sets `Instant.now()`, so it is a cannot-happen.
- **This supersedes the rating-wins rule from #356.** That change stopped the map and the Plan tab disagreeing, but resolved the disagreement toward the *stale* value — optimistic for the wrong reason, which is worse than the pessimism it replaced, because a wrong 4★ costs a drive. With the backend now serving the fresher source, the frontend's `resolveStandDown` becomes correct rather than merely consistent: `briefingScore` is the freshest verdict available, so preferring its rating is right.
- **A second bug underneath it: the freshness stamp itself was the row's birthday.** `persistToDb` only sets `evaluated_at` inside its `orElseGet` for a *new* row, so a slot re-evaluated for three days running still reported day one. Both DB-hydration sites (`EvaluationViewService` and `BriefingEvaluationService`) read `getEvaluatedAt()`; they now read `getUpdatedAt()`, which the entity documents as "when this row was last updated". The comparison is only sound on the last write — and this also corrects the "last calculated" stamp the UI shows.
- **`forecast_run_at` is a naive `LocalDateTime` recorded in `Europe/London`, not UTC.** Comparing it raw against an `Instant` is silently out by an hour through BST, which inverts the verdict for any pair written within an hour of each other — something the nightly cycle produces routinely. Zoning it is now behind a named helper with a test that fails if it is removed.
- Mutation-verified three ways: removing the freshness gate, dropping the London zoning, and reverting the stamp to `evaluated_at` each turn their own test red (the zoning also trips a pre-existing assertion that already pinned it).

### Fixed — the nightly cycle could never reach T+3, so a whole eligibility tier was dead code

- **`NightlyEligibilityPolicy` provides a T+3 SETTLED tier. Production has never once used it.** Measured over 14 days and 28 cycles: **zero** candidates at `days_ahead = 3`.
- **The cause is that the briefing is read a day after it is written.** Since V103 retired the standalone `daily_briefing` cron, the briefing is refreshed only at the *tail* of a pipeline cycle. The 01:00 nightly therefore consumes the briefing the previous afternoon's intraday cycle left behind, whose first date is by then yesterday — dropped by `BriefingCandidateCollector` as `PAST_DATE`. A four-date window `[D…D+3]` arrives at the next nightly as `[D−1…D+2]` and reaches only **T+2**.
- **The data shows the mechanism, not just the symptom.** The same 14 days recorded **5,684 candidates at `days_ahead = -1`** — one full quarter of every nightly candidate set spent on a date that had already happened — while the 14:00 intraday cycle showed **no `-1` bucket at all**, because it reads a briefing built the same morning. Two cycles, same code, opposite results, exactly as the ageing explanation predicts.
- **The window is now five dates**, so it still covers T+3 after ageing overnight. The fifth date is never rendered — `DailyBriefing.jsx` caps the grid at six solar events — and costs one extra region × event round of briefing gloss (~14 calls, ~£0.02 per refresh). It exists purely to survive the overnight gap.
- **The wasted `-1` quarter is left alone deliberately.** Past-date slots are dropped before any API call, so they cost disposition rows and nothing else. Reclaiming them means moving the briefing refresh to the *head* of the cycle, which doubles gloss and best-bet spend and risks serving a gloss-less briefing for the hours until the tail refresh lands — a change that belongs with the pipeline's sequencing, not here.
- Confirmable in production with the same query that found it: `SELECT days_ahead, count(*) FROM forecast_run_disposition WHERE created_at >= now() - interval '14 days' GROUP BY days_ahead`. A `days_ahead = 3` row appearing is the fix landing.

### Changed — Close to home is grouped by event window, server-derived, with a per-user radius
- **The derivation moved off the client entirely.** `closeToHome.js` (385 lines) and its 31 tests are **deleted**; the logic now lives in `CloseToHomeService`, joined on the location **FK** instead of a name string. Renaming a location used to empty the block silently for every user inside the radius.
