- Frontend: the map popup renders its header, stars, scores, event time, triage reason, and comfort line **instantly** from the slim row, then fetches the heavy detail (summary, tide, surge, inversion, wave, bluebell, dust badge, golden/blue-hour) by id with a skeleton and an in-memory role-scoped cache (cleared on logout alongside the SWR cache); a full forecast object (admin prompt-test harness) and sparse rows render inline without a fetch

### Added — ETag revalidation on read endpoints: unchanged polls return a cheap 304
- **The read GET endpoints (`/api/forecast`, `/api/forecast/history`, `/api/forecast/compare`, `/api/locations`, `/api/tides`, `/api/tides/stats`) now carry an ETag and `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`**, so an unchanged payload revalidates as a `304 Not Modified` instead of re-downloading the full body — compounding with the stale-while-revalidate cache: the cache paints instantly, and the background revalidation it fires comes back as a 304 when nothing changed. The browser's own HTTP cache reconstructs the body below the XHR layer, so axios still sees a full 200 and **no frontend change is needed** (#23)
- Implemented as a scoped `ShallowEtagHeaderFilter` (`HttpCachingConfig`) registered outermost on the read GET paths only — never the SSE streams (which must not be buffered) or writes. The `private, no-cache` header is set before the chain so Spring Security's default `no-store` (which would stop the browser caching the response at all) doesn't apply; writes keep `no-store`. The ETag is **body-derived**, which keeps it role-safe: a LITE request can't 304 into a cached PRO body because the differing body hashes to a different validator. Verified end-to-end through the full filter chain (`HttpCachingIntegrationTest`: ETag present, `no-cache` not `no-store`, 304 on unchanged, fresh 200 on changed, role-safe hash, non-whitelisted GET keeps `no-store`)
- **The outcome endpoints (`/api/outcome`, `/api/outcome/all`) are deliberately excluded** and keep `no-store`. Enabling an ETag requires letting the browser persist the body to its on-disk cache (that is how a 304 reconstructs it), which — unlike the localStorage SWR cache — can't be evicted from JS on logout; outcome responses carry user-authored free-text notes, so they stay out of the at-rest cache on a shared machine. Their revalidation saving would have been negligible anyway

### Added — Instant-paint map + Plan enrichments: stale-while-revalidate forecast cache
- **`useForecasts` now hydrates the last-seen forecast payload synchronously from a role-keyed localStorage cache**, so on refresh the map *and* the Plan tab's location-derived enrichments (drive times, hot-topic qualifying locations, seasonal features, the map-overlay handoff) paint instantly instead of waiting on the forecast + locations + outcomes fetch — the fetch then revalidates in the background and swaps in fresh data (same stale-while-revalidate pattern as the briefing cache). The cache stores the raw JSON API payloads (the `forecastsByDate` Maps are rebuilt on hydrate via an extracted pure `buildLocations`) and is role-keyed because forecast scores differ LITE vs PRO. Guardrails so the optimisation never jars or misleads: a failed *background* revalidation keeps the stale map on screen rather than blanking it with an error, and a revalidation over already-painted data never flashes the loading skeleton — but a failed *user-initiated* refresh (Run Forecast, drive-time change, manual re-run) still surfaces an error/retry, since an explicit action expects feedback; and an empty-but-valid cache shows the loading skeleton rather than flashing the "no forecasts loaded yet" empty state (#22)

### Changed — HTTP delivery: cache the shell short, the hashed assets forever; load Turnstile on demand
- **Hashed build assets are now cached hard for a year (`immutable`), while `index.html` and the other unhashed root files revalidate (`no-cache`)** — Vite content-hashes every asset filename, so a changed bundle gets a new URL and can be cached permanently; the shell must revalidate so a deploy's new hashes are picked up on the next visit. Previously `index.html` inherited the same 30-day `immutable` policy as the assets via the catch-all `location /`, which could serve a stale shell for up to 30 days after a deploy. An unchanged shell is now a cheap 304, not a re-download (#24)
- **The Cloudflare Turnstile script is loaded on demand by `TurnstileWidget` when an auth page mounts, instead of eagerly in `index.html` on every page load** — the common case is a returning, signed-in user who never sees a CAPTCHA, so the third-party request is now off the load path entirely for them. The widget already polled for `window.turnstile`, so it picks the API up as soon as the injected script finishes loading (well within its existing 10s budget) (#20)

### Changed — Load-path request hygiene (fewer/deferred boot requests)
- **`getAllEvaluationScores` now goes through the shared `apiClient`** instead of a raw `fetch()` with a hand-attached token, so it participates in the single-flight 401→refresh retry — a token-boundary refresh no longer silently drops the Plan tab's batch scores (#19)
- **The two long-lived SSE streams (health status + run notifications) are deferred until after first paint** via a new `useAfterFirstPaint` hook (idle-callback with a timeout fallback), so they don't compete with the critical forecast/briefing fetches during boot; `useHealthStatus` gained an `enabled` gate (#17)
- **Aurora status is fetched once and shared** — a new `AuroraStatusProvider` wraps the authenticated app and does the single fetch + 5-minute poll + focus refetch; `AuroraBanner`, `MapView`, and `JobRunsMetricsView` all read it via the unchanged `useAuroraStatus()` hook instead of each firing their own request/poll (#18)
- **Astro heatmap conditions load one wave earlier** — the Plan tab no longer gates the per-date `getAstroConditions` fan-out behind a separate `getAstroAvailableDates` round-trip; it fetches directly for the briefing's upcoming dates and tolerates empty responses (#21)

### Changed — /api/forecast no longer re-loads forecast_evaluation per location for the cached-only rows
- **`getForecasts` used `EvaluationViewService.forDateRange`, which loads the latest `forecast_evaluation` row per enabled location and merges everything — but `getForecasts` already has its own latest forecast rows (from the dedup query) and only keeps the *cached-only* views.** So that whole per-location `forecast_evaluation` re-query + merge was wasted work on the map's primary endpoint, every request. A new `EvaluationViewService.cachedOnlyViewsForDateRange(start, end, types, locations)` builds the same `CACHED_EVALUATION` views from the cache alone (safe because `mergeToView` gives cached results priority, so an empty forecast map yields identical cached views), skipping the N+1 forecast query entirely and reusing the caller's already-loaded enabled locations (so no repeat `findAllEnabled`)
- `forDateRange` is unchanged for its other caller (`BriefingEvaluationController`, the Map tab) — the per-location load + merge were extracted into shared `loadLatestForecasts` / `buildViews` helpers and `loadCachedEvaluations` now takes the caller's locations instead of re-querying them
- Tests: `EvaluationViewServiceTest` gains a `cachedOnlyViewsForDateRange` case asserting it returns the cached views **without** any per-location `forecast_evaluation` query or a repeat `findAllEnabled`; `forDateRange` behaviour and the `getForecasts` cached-row surfacing are both still covered

### Added — Gentle "just refreshed" fade on the Plan tab
- **When the instant-paint cache revalidates and swaps in a genuinely newer briefing, the Plan card now settles in with a subtle 450ms opacity fade** (`animate-briefing-refresh`, a soft 0.6→1 dip) instead of the values snapping in place. It fires **only** when `generatedAt` actually changes — never on first load, and never on the common same-data revalidation (which leaves `generatedAt` untouched, so the effect doesn't even re-run). Honours `prefers-reduced-motion`, and the swap still reconciles in place (no remount, so expanded cards / selected day are preserved). Tests: fade fires on a newer briefing, and does not on first load or a same-data revalidation

### Changed — /api/forecast backend scale wins at 200+ locations (fewer queries, less per-row compute)
- **`hibernate.default_batch_fetch_size: 32`** (added to every profile's `spring.jpa.properties`) — `LocationEntity`'s three eager `@ElementCollection`s (solar-event / tide / location types) previously loaded with one `SELECT` per location; they now batch into `IN`-queries, sharply cutting the query count on `/api/forecast` and `/api/locations` as the roster grows
- **Marine sea-state resolved in one batched query, not per row.** `ForecastDtoMapper.toDtoList` now preloads the whole window's `marine_wave` rows once (`findByEvaluationDateBetween`) and indexes them by `locationId|date|eventType`, so mapping a coastal row is a map lookup instead of a `marine_wave` `SELECT` per coastal row — one query replaces N. The single-row `toDto(entity, isLite)` path is unchanged (still a direct lookup)
- **Lunar classification memoised per date.** The mapper recomputed `classifyTide` + `getMoonPhase` for every row though they depend only on the date; they're now computed once per distinct date and reused across that date's rows (solar-window compute is left per-row — its lat/lon/date key is unique per row, so memoising wouldn't help)
- **Bulk and single-row wave paths share one coastal predicate** (`isCoastalWaveSlot`) so they stay equivalent by construction: a location edited from coastal to inland (its `tideType` cleared) whose stale `marine_wave` rows still exist resolves to no sea-state on both paths — caught and fixed via an adversarial review of this change
- Tests: `ForecastDtoMapperTest` gains a batched-wave-preload case (two rows → one query, never per-row), a reclassified-inland equivalence case, and a lunar case proving one compute per **distinct** date; all existing mapper/controller behaviour preserved
- Deliberately deferred to a focused follow-up: the sparse cached-only path (`toSparseDto`) still resolves waves per row (J2 batched only the rich-row path); deduping the 3× `findAllEnabled` per request (low-value once batch-fetch is set); the larger win of `EvaluationViewService.forDateRange` re-loading `forecast_evaluation` per location inside `getForecasts` (which only keeps the cached-only views); and `non_null` serialization (marginal post-gzip and a response-shape change)

### Added — Instant-paint Plan tab: stale-while-revalidate briefing cache
- **On refresh the Plan tab paints its last briefing immediately instead of showing a skeleton.** A new `utils/swrCache.js` (a tiny localStorage stale-while-revalidate cache: `readSwrCache`/`writeSwrCache`/`clearSwrCache`, entries namespaced under `photocast_swr:` and stamped `{ts,value}`) lets `DailyBriefing` hydrate its `briefing`/`loading` state synchronously from the last cached payload, then revalidate via the normal `getDailyBriefing()` fetch and swap in fresh data. Best Bet / Hot Topics / the region grid appear with no network wait; combined with the earlier Plan-ungate this makes a refresh feel instant
- **Cache is role-keyed and cleared on session end.** The key is `briefing:<role>` so a LITE session never reads a PRO-tier briefing, and both `logout` **and** the session-expiry path (`AuthContext` `handleExpired`, the most common session end) call `clearSwrCache`, so a cached payload never leaks to the next account on a shared browser
- **Bounded and poison-safe.** Cache entries older than 12h are ignored (so a long-stale plan isn't shown even briefly — the briefing regenerates every ~8–10h); an empty/`204` revalidation is ignored rather than overwriting the good cache or blanking the view; and reads/writes are quota-, private-mode-, and corrupt-JSON-safe (fail to a normal fetch). Reviewed with an adversarial multi-agent pass; the four low/nit findings (session-expiry clear, empty-response poisoning, and two coverage gaps) are all addressed with tests
- **Tests** — `swrCache.test.js` (round-trip, role-independence, staleness, corruption, selective clear, quota-safe) plus `DailyBriefing` cache-hit-paints-instantly / role-mismatch-miss / write-on-fetch / keep-on-error / no-poison-on-empty, and `AuthContext` cache-clear on both logout and session-expiry

## [v2.16.5] - 2026-07-24

### Changed — Faster refresh: code-split the map/admin bundles and render the Plan tab immediately
- **The Plan view no longer waits for the map's data to load.** `App.jsx` gated the entire `<main>` behind a single `useForecasts` `loading` flag (forecast + locations + outcomes), so Best Bet / Hot Topics / the region grid — which are driven by the independent *briefing* fetch — didn't paint until all the map-oriented data resolved. `DailyBriefing` now mounts immediately on the default Plan tab, firing its briefing fetch at t=0 with its own skeleton and tolerating an empty `locations` list (drive-time/type enrichment fills in when forecasts arrive). Only the Map tab keeps the forecast loading/error/empty gating. Side benefit: the Plan tab is now resilient to a forecast-fetch failure instead of the whole app blocking on it
- **`MapView`, `MapOverlay`, and `ManageView` are now `React.lazy` behind `Suspense`.** Production was a single 1,277 kB (360 kB gzip) chunk with no code-splitting; the Leaflet stack and the admin-only Manage view (plus recharts) shipped to every session even though Plan is the default tab and the map is a drill-down. Initial JS on a Plan refresh drops from **360 kB → ~124 kB gzip (~66% smaller)**; the Leaflet stack (~80 kB gzip incl. CSS) loads only when the map opens, and `ManageView` + **recharts (107 kB gzip)** load only when an admin opens Manage
- **`vite.config.js` `manualChunks`** splits `react` / `leaflet` / `recharts` into separate cacheable vendor chunks so they download in parallel and survive app-only redeploys (verified: five distinct JS chunks, no bundler warnings)
- **`nginx.conf` now sets `gzip_types`** for `application/javascript`, `text/css`, `application/json`, and SVG (nginx's built-in default only gzips `text/html`, so the hashed JS/CSS bundle was previously served uncompressed at the origin). `text/event-stream` is deliberately excluded so SSE is never buffered; API JSON is already gzipped by the backend and nginx skips already-encoded responses

## [v2.16.4] - 2026-07-24

### Fixed — Recorded-outcome map markers used a future date window
- **The map's recorded outcomes almost never appeared.** `useForecasts` fetched actual outcomes over a *forward* `[today, today+7]` window, but a recorded outcome is a **past** observation — a photographer rating a sunrise/sunset they already saw (`ActualOutcomeEntity.outcomeDate` is always historical). Querying all-future dates returned essentially nothing, so the outcome markers/badges were perpetually empty. The window now looks **backwards** — `[today−7, today]` — matching the forecast payload's own `today−7` past edge (`ForecastController`), so every outcome lines up with a date the map already renders (both sides compute their date basis in UTC). Pre-existing bug, untouched by the batched-outcomes change (#278) which preserved the original window; surfaced by an adversarial review of that change. Added a `useForecasts` test asserting the fetch window is backward-looking, spans 7 days, and still attaches its outcomes.

## [v2.16.3] - 2026-07-21

### Changed — Away days collapse into a slim band below the full grid (Plan-screen signal cleanup A3b)
- **Travel/away days no longer eat a full greyed grid column.** An away day (no forecast run) previously rendered as a whole column of "✈️ Away" cells plus a header badge, taking the same width as a real forecast day — so two of four columns could be dead. Away days are now dropped from the grid's columns and shown as a slim band below it (`✈ Tomorrow–Thursday · away — no forecast generated`), with **consecutive away days collapsed into one band**; the reclaimed width goes to the real forecast days (the cramped ones carrying every actual cell). The compact summary strip is unchanged — it still shows its away pills. An open drill-down whose date gets reclassified as away (on a briefing refresh) is now dismissed rather than left orphaned. This clears the last item from Change A.

### Added — Confidence marker on drill-down and mobile region pills (Change B follow-up)
- **The shared `VerdictPill` now carries the confidence channel**, so the drill-down region rows and the mobile region cards read on the same channel as the grid cells and Best Bet: a low-confidence (provisional) region verdict appends the small quiet marker. Only rated verdicts (Worth it / Maybe) are marked — a poor/awaiting pill is already its own signal — and the pill stays structurally unchanged whenever there's no marker. Wired at the three region-level call sites; the per-location slot pills stay clean, since confidence is a region property. This clears the last deferred Change B item.

### Added — Certainty vocabulary on hot topics (Change B follow-up)
- **Each hot topic now names what KIND of certainty it is** — a small, quiet word on the pill: **almanac**, **forecast**, or **chance**. Hot topics differ in epistemic kind, not just degree: a spring tide is astronomically fixed, a snow forecast decays with the horizon, and noctilucent cloud can't be forecast at all. Rendering them at equal weight implied a false equivalence; the word restores the distinction. This is the second, orthogonal axis to the horizon confidence channel (Change B) — derived purely from the topic type client-side (no backend change). Tides + astronomical events (king/spring tides, supermoon, meteors, equinox) read "almanac"; weather-driven topics read "forecast"; NLC reads "chance" (the window is fixed, the display isn't forecastable). Word-only (no colour semantics — the word carries it, per "legible in words, not just colour"), with a hover gloss; it drops on narrow viewports to keep the pill's detail sentence full-width.

## [v2.16.2] - 2026-07-21

### Added — Durable per-evaluation forecast confidence (V127)
- **Each forecast evaluation now records a horizon-derived confidence** — `forecast_evaluation.confidence` (HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW from the row's `days_ahead`), the durable, per-row counterpart to Change B's spread-aware region confidence. A single evaluation has no cross-location spread, so this is horizon-only (`ConfidenceDeriver.fromHorizon`), stored as the enum name to match `pipeline_run_pick.confidence`. Populated at the sole evaluation write site, so every new row carries it regardless of model. The column is additive, nullable, and read by nothing as a gate — a durable record for calibrating how far-ahead forecasts pan out against recorded outcomes.

### Added — Confidence as one uniform, quiet channel on the Plan screen (Change B)
- **A far-horizon "Worth it" now reads visibly more provisional than a same-day one.** The Plan screen previously rendered a `4.0★` for tonight and one three days out identically, though the far one is far less reliable — horizon confidence was invisible. A new confidence channel fixes that, layered **alongside** the star (which stays as the sky-quality signal, unchanged), never replacing it.
- **Backend-derived, spread-aware.** Each region carries a `confidence` (high/medium/low) derived server-side from the forecast horizon (the dominant term) plus the region's rating spread and coverage, so it reflects real model agreement — not just days-out. It rides the existing briefing cache (no migration) and falls back to a horizon-only tier client-side if the field is ever absent.
- **One treatment everywhere.** Grid cells dim their verdict fill as confidence drops and show a small "provisional" marker on the low tier; the Best Bet card's low-confidence state now routes through the same channel; the summary strip's day-pills carry the same marker. The star/quality badge is never touched.
- Deferred to a follow-up (intentionally): the hot-topic certainty *vocabulary* (a separate axis — aurora is a live detection, tides are almanac-certain, seasonal is a forecast) and the drill-down/mobile verdict pills.

### Changed — Aurora banner now gates on clear skies (Plan-screen signal cleanup A1)
- **The top-slot aurora alert no longer fires when every dark-sky location is overcast.** A `MODERATE` "aurora tonight" that can't be seen anywhere was noise in the most valuable position on the screen; NLC already gates the same way (`active && clearTonight`), and aurora now aligns with it. The banner is suppressed when `darkSkyLocationCount > 0 && clearLocationCount === 0` — **unless** it's a major storm (`STRONG`+), which is rare and worth surfacing even under cloud (gaps open, people travel). Fails **open** when the clear count is unknown (`clearLocationCount == null`), so missing data never hides a real alert.
- The existing "All locations overcast" sub-note now renders only for the surviving `STRONG`+ case, so it reads honestly rather than contradicting a banner nobody can act on.

### Changed — Best Bet recommendation renders in full, no clamp (Plan-screen signal cleanup A2)
- **The headline recommendation is no longer truncated.** The Best Bet / Also Good detail paragraph previously clamped to two lines behind a "Read more ▾" toggle — the one element you most want read to completion was hidden behind an interaction. The clamp and toggle are gone; the detail wraps fully with `text-wrap: pretty`. The copy is already written as complete sentences, so nothing is clipped mid-word. If unbounded height ever becomes a concern, cap the *source copy* in the briefing payload rather than clipping in CSS.

### Changed — One verb for "go to the map" across the Plan screen (copy & a11y tidy C2)
- **The same gesture is now worded one way.** "View on map →" (aurora + NLC banners) and "🗺 View on map →" (Best Bet) all become **"Show on map →"**, matching the compact strip pills that already used it. The overlay footer's **"Open the full Map tab →"** stays deliberately distinct — it's a different destination (the whole Map tab, not the in-place overlay).

### Changed — Dropped the model name from the user-facing planner stamp (copy & a11y tidy C1)
- **The planner header no longer reads "11h ago · by Sonnet" for photographers.** The model name is developer-facing implementation detail that means nothing to a user, so the "by &lt;model&gt;" span is now gated to ADMIN only — the age ("Updated 11h ago") still shows for everyone, and admins keep the model label for internal debugging.

### Changed — Raised contrast on informational fine print (copy & a11y tidy C3)
- **Fine print that carries real information is a step brighter.** The hot-topic "science showing" fact note moves from muted (0.42α) to secondary (0.66α), and the per-region science gloss headline from 0.5α to 0.62α — both were below comfortable contrast for body-size text on the dark surface. Decorative-only labels stay quiet, and the gloss *bodies* (InfoTip card, summary-strip tooltip) already sat at the secondary tier. Info tips remain tap-to-toggle (already reachable on touch via the shared `InfoTip`), so no hover-only affordance is left stranded.

### Changed — Full briefing grid pools the poor rows behind a reveal (Plan-screen signal cleanup A3a)
- **Rated regions lead; poor-only regions collapse behind a toggle.** Expanded, the grid was mostly dead cells — a user scanned every region row to find the two or three worth acting on. Now only regions with at least one Worth-it / Maybe cell render by default; the rest fold behind a full-width `+N regions · all poor ▾` / `Hide poor regions ▴` toggle (the same "poor conditions" divider idea the drill-down already uses, applied one level up to the region rows). A region is "poor" when every one of its evaluated cells is stand-down/awaiting — keyed on the display verdict, since the quality slider was retired and every tier now renders. An all-poor week (nothing rated to lead with) shows every row instead of hiding them behind an empty grid.

### Fixed — Review follow-ups on the Plan-screen signal cleanup
- **The C3 contrast bump now covers both per-region gloss headlines.** The first pass raised the aurora expanded-card science gloss headline (0.5α → 0.62α) but missed the structurally identical bluebell/hot-topic headline, which stayed below comfortable contrast; it now matches, so no informational gloss headline is left at the dim tier.
- **The aurora clear-sky gate never suppresses an admin simulation.** A simulated alert is an admin preview tool that must render regardless of weather, so the gate now applies only to real alerts (`allOvercast && !isMajorStorm && !isSimulated`).

## [v2.16.1] - 2026-07-17

### Fixed — Health banner stuck DOWN on tab-return; the status SSE stream was stranded on an expired token
- **Returning to an already-open tab showed a persistent red "Service is temporarily unavailable" banner (health pill DOWN) that only a full page reload cleared.** The banner is driven by the `/api/status/stream` SSE connection, which authenticated with the access token captured once in a frozen `AuthContext` closure (`getToken: () => token`). The axios 401 interceptor refreshes the access token into `localStorage` only — never into `AuthContext` state — so once the 24h access token expired, every reconnect re-sent the dead token → 401 → `EventSource` CLOSED (fatal) → a 5 s retry loop that re-sent the same expired token forever. A reload re-read the already-refreshed token from `localStorage`, which is why refreshing always cleared it.
- **`useHealthStatus`** now reads the freshest token from `localStorage` at each (re)connect instead of the closure, so a reconnecting stream is never stranded on a stale token.
- **`axiosClient`** dispatches `goldenhour:token-refreshed` after a successful silent refresh, and **`AuthContext`** listens for it and re-syncs its in-memory `token`/`refreshToken` — closing the state-vs-localStorage divergence for every consumer and making the health stream re-subscribe promptly.
- **`createEventSource`** closes the previous `EventSource` before reconnecting, so a stale source can't keep firing `onError` or race the new connection.
- **`DOWN_GRACE_MS` widened 6 s → 12 s** so an ordinary healthy reconnect (which lands just after the 5 s retry delay) no longer flashes the banner before the status event clears it; the trade is that a genuine outage surfaces a few seconds later.
- Tests: new regression coverage across all three layers (axios dispatches `token-refreshed` and carries the fresh token on retry; `AuthContext` re-syncs on the event; `createEventSource` reads a fresh token on reconnect and closes the previous source). Full frontend suite green, ESLint clean.

### Added — Colour-coded the Best bet / Also good picks, and carried that identity into the compact briefing strip
- **The two headline picks now read as a matched pair.** The Also good card gains a periwinkle left-border and label (`--color-pick-also` `#7C8DD6`, a new shared token), peering the Best bet card's existing GO-green accent — it previously used a neutral border and muted label, so at a glance the second pick looked like chrome rather than a recommendation. Low confidence still mutes both cards to neutral, unchanged.
- **The same two colours flow down into the compact day-tile region run.** A chip whose region *is* the Best bet or Also good is drawn in the pick's colour (text + dotted underline at 60% alpha), weight 600, with a leading `◎` marker so the pick is identifiable without relying on colour alone (the marker is `aria-hidden` — a decorative redundancy for the colour, not a screen-reader announcement). The picks are floated to the front of the run (best, then also); the remaining regions keep their roll-up order.
- **Matched by region identity, not list position.** Each pick is resolved to its region name — the stable id shared by the `bestBets` pick payload and the grid roll-up (`buildSummaryPills`) — so a pick shows its colour on *every* day it appears rated, not only its headline day. That multi-day behaviour is an emergent property of the day-independent match, matching the handoff's stated intent.
- **CSS specificity guard.** The existing per-tile hover tint (`[data-peak="go"]:hover` → green, `maybe` → amber) is now scoped `:not([data-pick])` so it can't repaint a picked chip's colour on hover; picked chips instead only `brightness(1.14)`. Without the guard a periwinkle "also" chip on a GO tile would flip to green on hover.
- **PRO/ADMIN only**, gated on the same `isPro` + `bestBets` condition as the Best bet banner itself — LITE users see a redacted banner, so colouring their strip chips would point at cards they can't see.
- Tests: `BriefingSummaryStrip` gains a marker/`data-pick`/plain-chip case; `DailyBriefing` gains best-first/also-second ordering and a LITE no-highlight gate, and its "two picks, different visual weight" test now pins the periwinkle Also good accent the handoff makes final. 1718 frontend tests green, ESLint clean; reviewed against the HTML handoff with an adversarial multi-agent pass (0 confirmed findings).

## [v2.16.0] - 2026-07-17

### Changed — Deduplicated the model-test fan-out, the colour-type predicate, the gloss stats, and a copied prompt method
- **`ModelTestService`'s evaluate-and-persist block existed four times** (`runTest`, `runTestForLocation`, `rerunTest`, `rerunTestDeterministic`), and each method *also* carried a copy of the weather-fetch-failure fan-out — eight loops over `TEST_MODELS` in one class. The four success copies differed only in log wording and in where they read the row identity from (region/location/date/type, or a previous result being replayed); the two failed-result builders differed only in the same way. All of it now goes through one `ResultSlot` (the row identity, however sourced), one `RunTally`, one `evaluateModels`, one `recordAllModelsFailed`, and one `buildFailedResult`. 842 → 752 lines. Log wording is unified to the richest form (it now always names the region); the determinism-rerun flavour text is gone, and that context is already on the run record.
- **`hasColourTypes` existed four times and had already drifted** — identical private copies in `ForecastCommandExecutor`, `ModelTestService` and `PromptTestService`, plus an inline lambda in `ForceSubmitBatchService` that was the only one to null-guard the type set. It moves to `LocationEntity.hasColourTypes()`, beside its sibling `supportsTargetType`, keeping the **union** of the four behaviours: the null guard is retained (unreachable while the field defaults to an empty set, but no caller loses anything). Its tests move to `LocationEntityTest` and gain the null-set, mixed-type and per-colour-type cases none of the copies covered.
- **`BriefingGlossService` recomputed the rating aggregation inline** — the last of four copies of the "high ≥ 4, medium == 3, mean to 1 dp" rule, and the only one that skipped `RatingValidator`. It now calls `BriefingRatingStats.compute`. The arithmetic is provably identical for in-range data (same predicates, same `Math.round(avg * 10.0) / 10.0`); the difference is that an out-of-range rating is now skipped and logged rather than inflating the counts and the mean that are fed to Haiku as prompt input.
- **`CoastalPromptBuilder.buildUserMessage(4-arg)` was a character-identical copy** of the method it overrode and is deleted. Provably behaviour-preserving: the class overrides neither `buildUserMessage(AtmosphericData)` nor `getPromptSuffix()`, so the `super.` in the copy and the `this.` in the parent resolved to the same methods. `PromptGoldenMasterTest`, which pins the whole assembled prompt byte-for-byte across five archetypes, is green — the prompt is unchanged.
- 4490 tests green, JaCoCo per-class and SpotBugs passing, checkstyle 0.

### Fixed — Aurora viewline re-downloaded a ~900 KB NOAA grid per page load; aurora batch ignored its own kill switch
- **The viewline bypassed the cache its own Javadoc claimed to use.** `NoaaSwpcClient.fetchViewline` ran a bare HTTP GET on every call while documenting "Reuses the OVATION cache (5-minute TTL)" — so `/api/aurora/viewline`, which the map calls on **every authenticated page load**, re-downloaded the ~900 KB OVATION grid each time, unbounded in user traffic. It now caches for 5 minutes like its siblings. Only the *pre-cap* viewline is cached: `applyKpCap` is a pure function of (viewline, Kp) and still runs per call, so a changing Kp moves the line on a cache hit — pinned by a test. It also now fails open like every sibling fetch (a NOAA outage keeps the last good overlay instead of blanking it).
- **`aurora_batch_evaluation` declared `aurora.enabled` as its `config_source` and was never gated by it.** `DynamicSchedulerService` matched a hardcoded `AURORA_JOB_KEYS = Set.of("aurora_polling")` instead of reading the column, so the only thing between "aurora switched off" and a nightly NOAA fetch + Anthropic Batch spend was a PAUSED seed row that one Resume click undoes — with no test asserting otherwise. Two layers now close it: the gate is driven by `config_source` (an unrecognised value is a no-op, not a crash — the column is free text and prod rows are hand-editable), and `doSubmitAuroraBatch` self-gates on `aurora.enabled` like `AuroraPollingJob.poll()` already did. The self-gate is the layer that actually stops the spend; the status makes the Scheduler UI tell the truth. The three tests that exercised aurora batch submission passed with a mocked `isEnabled()` returning `false` — which was itself the proof there was no gate.
- **V126** adds `status_before_config_disable`. Without it, generalising the gate would have *created* a spend bug: `aurora_batch_evaluation` is deliberately seeded PAUSED, and the old "DISABLED_BY_CONFIG → ACTIVE" restore would have promoted it to ACTIVE on the first `aurora.enabled` off→on toggle, starting nightly batch submissions nobody asked for. The pre-disable status is now remembered and restored (defaulting to ACTIVE when absent, preserving `aurora_polling`'s existing behaviour). As a side-effect an admin's manual pause now survives a config toggle, where it was previously silently reset to ACTIVE.
- **The fail-open branch had zero tests** — the one keeping the aurora banner alive through a NOAA outage, across all five fetch methods. It was untestable by construction: reaching it needs an *expired* cache and `isFresh` read the wall clock. `NoaaSwpcClient` now takes the existing `Clock` bean (already injected this way in `ForecastScoreWriter`), and 11 new characterization tests pin caching, TTL expiry, stale-retention on outage, and cold-failure for the OVATION/Kp/alerts/viewline paths.
- 4 new `DynamicSchedulerServiceTest` cases pin the V126 status machine (PAUSED is remembered and restored, never promoted; an admin pause survives an off/on cycle; an unrecognised `config_source` is a no-op). An adversarial review flagged that the restore branch — the guard against the promotion described above — was itself unpinned; a mutation check confirms the two new tests fail if it reverts to a hardcoded ACTIVE.
- Known and accepted: `fetchOvation` and `fetchViewline` now cache the same OVATION URL separately, so steady state is 2 downloads per 5 minutes rather than 1. That is bounded and a large net win over the previous *per-page-load* download; sharing one fetch would mean re-parsing ~900 KB on each read, so it is left as a possible follow-up.

### Changed — Design-pattern quick wins: notification Observer, extracted parser, one date table, tier-based pricing
- **Notifications are an Observer.** `EmailNotificationService`, `PushoverNotificationService` and `MacOsToastNotificationService` had an identical `notify(...)` signature but no shared interface, so `ForecastService` hand-dispatched to all three — copy-pasted at two call sites. They now implement `NotificationChannel`, and `NotificationDispatcher` injects `List<NotificationChannel>` and loops: adding a channel is one new class, no call-site edits. **This fixed a real bug**: the old code wrapped all three sends in a *single* try/catch, so an email failure silently suppressed the Pushover and toast sends. The dispatcher isolates each channel. `ForecastService` keeps its own backstop around `dispatch` — the dispatcher isolating channels from each other and the forecast being immune to the notification layer are different guarantees, and the second one predates this change.
- **Parsing left the strategy.** `ForecastResultHandler` and `SkyRatingEvalBatchService` each reached into the `Map<EvaluationModel, EvaluationStrategy>`, pulled the HAIKU entry, and downcast it to `ClaudeEvaluationStrategy` purely to borrow its JSON parser — throwing `IllegalStateException` if the wiring ever changed. Both downcasts are gone: the ~230 lines of patterns and parse methods moved to a `SunsetEvaluationParser` component that all three collaborators inject. `ClaudeEvaluationStrategy` drops from 433 to ~150 lines and is now only prompt → API → delegate-parse. The two obsolete tests that pinned the downcast's `IllegalStateException` are deleted (the guard they tested no longer exists by design); the 33 parsing tests moved verbatim to `SunsetEvaluationParserTest`.
- **One date table.** `RunType.defaultDateRange(today)` is now the single source of truth, replacing duplicate `switch (runType)` blocks in `ForecastCommandFactory` and `PromptTestService` — which had **drifted**. ⚠️ **Behaviour change (deliberate):** the prompt-test harness's LONG_TERM sweep was T+3..T+7, two days past `FORECAST_HORIZON_DAYS` and past Gate 4's "T+4 and beyond is never evaluated" policy — it was prompt-testing horizons production never scores. It now matches production at T+3..T+5, so a LONG_TERM prompt test makes ~40% fewer real Claude calls. `PromptTestService` keeps its own "not supported for prompt tests" guard. The enum's own javadoc ("T+3 through T+7") was stale against *both* implementations and is fixed.
- **Pricing by tier.** `CostCalculator` repeated the model→rate-tier mapping in four near-identical switches, each ending `default -> sonnetRate` — so an unmapped model would be silently billed at Sonnet rates. The mapping now lives once on `EvaluationModel.pricingTier()`, and the four rate lookups switch on the tier exhaustively: no `default`, so a new model cannot compile without declaring its tier. WILDLIFE maps to `FREE` (0.0) rather than falling through to Sonnet — reachability-verified as a no-op today (WILDLIFE never carries tokens), so this closes a latent hazard rather than changing a bill.
- **Wildlife dispatch is type-safe.** Two `instanceof NoOpEvaluationStrategy` checks became `getEvaluationModel() == WILDLIFE`, which the `EvaluationStrategy` interface already exposes and which survives decoration by `MetricsLoggingDecorator` (the `instanceof` would silently break if a command's strategy were ever wrapped). `instanceof` is null-tolerant and a bare `getEvaluationModel()` call is not, so the executor keeps an explicit null check — `ForecastCommand.strategy` is documented nullable and the sibling check in the factory guards the same way.
- **The date table had a *third* copy — in the frontend.** `PromptTestView` carried its own `{ desc: 'T+3 to T+7', days: 5 }`, which the backend narrowing turned stale: the confirmation dialog that gates a real-money Claude run quoted the wrong date range and a day count 67% over actual. (Its sibling `ModelSelectionView` was already correct at T+3–T+5, because it mirrored production — the drift had simply been on the other foot.) Both views now derive from one `utils/runTypeRanges.js` mirroring `RunType.defaultDateRange`, and a new `runTypeRanges.test.js` pins the numbers, which nothing did before. Caught by the adversarial review pass, not by the build.
- Tests: new `NotificationDispatcherTest` pins the every-channel and per-channel-isolation guarantees the old dispatch lacked; new `SunsetEvaluationParserTest` (33 tests, moved verbatim). 4454 backend tests green (clean build — a stale `target/` from another branch will otherwise re-run deleted test classes), 1715 frontend tests green, JaCoCo per-class and SpotBugs passing, checkstyle 0.

### Changed — ForecastEvaluationEntity decomposed into embedded value objects; JPA entities no longer carry Jackson annotations
- The 66-field `forecast_evaluation` entity had absorbed every feature since V1 as flat nullable columns. Its six feature clusters are now `@Embeddable` value objects — `TideDetails`, `DirectionalCloudDetails`, `CloudApproachDetails`, `StormSurgeDetails`, `InversionDetails`, `TriageDetails` — each with a `from(...)` factory that absorbs the null-guard ternaries previously copy-pasted through `ForecastService.buildEntity` (the tide block alone was six `tide != null ? … : null` lines; it is now one `TideDetails.from(data.tide())`). **Zero schema change**: all 64 column definitions (names, precision, length, TEXT) verified byte-identical against the previous entity — important because the test schema is Hibernate-generated (`ddl-auto: create-drop`), so a column-name typo would be invisible to tests.
- Hibernate materialises an all-null embeddable as `null`, so "this row has no tide/surge/triage context" is now one null check instead of six; readers that want field-level access without null-guards use each type's `orEmpty()` view (`ForecastDtoMapper`). The one JPQL reference (`e.tideAligned` in `countTideAlignedByTargetType`) tracks to `e.tide.aligned`.
- The six value objects are read-only (no `@Setter`) — Hibernate populates them by field access, and nothing else needs to mutate them. That also makes the shared all-null instance behind `orEmpty()` safe to hand out: an adversarial review flagged the first cut's mutable singleton as a latent cross-request corruption hazard, and immutability removes it at the type level rather than by convention.
- **De-Jacksoned the entities**: `@JsonIgnore`/`@JsonProperty` stripped from `ForecastEvaluationEntity`, `ActualOutcomeEntity`, and `PromptTestRunEntity` — persistence classes no longer know about the API contract. The one **live** raw-entity serialization this exposed is fixed: `OutcomeController` GET/POST returned `ActualOutcomeEntity` directly; both now return the new `ActualOutcomeDto`, whose field set matches the previous JSON byte-for-byte (entity fields minus the `location` relation, plus `locationName`), so the frontend contract is unchanged.
- Tests: new `ForecastEvaluationDetailsTest` pins every factory's null/populated/partial contracts (including `StormSurgeDetails`' ranges-without-breakdown rule and `CloudApproachDetails`' empty-slots flattening); entity-touching assertions in `ForecastServiceTest`/`ForecastDtoMapperTest`/`EvaluationViewServiceTest`/`ForecastEvaluationRepositoryTest` re-pinned through the clusters. 189 affected tests green, checkstyle clean.

### Changed — Also Good floor lowered 3.5 → 3.0, and a missing Also Good now explains itself
- **The floor.** Pick 2's quality bar was 3.5 in both tiers of the advisor's selection rule. Five consecutive production cycles (14–16 Jul) show the best regional `claudeAverageRating` *anywhere* peaking at 3.0–3.2 — the advisor's own reasoning reads "None clear 3.5" and, on 16 Jul, "not within 0.5 of 3.1, and below 3.5 threshold". The bar was set above the working range of the rating distribution, so Also Good was near-unreachable rather than selective. Lowered to 3.0 on the product judgment that a 3-star slot is worth a look. Replaying the five captured rollups against the new bar: the three runs whose peaks were 3.0–3.2 gain a same-slot Pick 2, and the two genuinely poor runs (peaks 2.5–2.7) still correctly decline — the bar now discriminates instead of blocking everything.
- The scarcity tiebreak's "usual quality bar (>= 3.5)" tracks to 3.0 so the prompt names one bar, not two. The Pick 1 ranking guidance (">3.5 is promising, >4.0 is excellent") is deliberately unchanged — that is a preference hint, not a floor, and the two are different concerns. `BriefingBestBetAdvisor.PICK_TWO_RATING_FLOOR` mirrors the new value for diagnostics only; the prompt remains the single source of truth.
- No test changed, because the floor never had test coverage and cannot usefully get any — it lives in a prompt text block, where a unit test could only assert the string contains "3.0". The real validation is the `/api/admin/advisor-replay` harness (captured `apiCallLogId` + `candidatePrompt`), which diffs a candidate prompt against the live one on a real rollup.
- **The diagnostics.** A flat week yields a Best Bet and no Also Good — which, from the outside, is indistinguishable from a silent failure. Nothing in the log or the UI said which it was, and answering it took a `pipeline_run_pick` query against production.
- `BriefingBestBetAdvisor` now emits one `[BEST-BET PICK2]` INFO line per cycle: the best rating available, how many rated slots clear the Also Good floor, and the top 3 candidates by rating (`event|region=rating(n=ratedCount)`). Zero clearing the floor reads as "no Also Good is possible" — the honest-silence case is now legible without a database, and a week of these lines is what tells you whether the 3.0 bar is calibrated.
- The Planner's best-bet banner renders a "② NO ALSO GOOD" companion card when a fresh cycle produced a single pick, filling the empty grid column that previously just looked broken. Deliberately scoped to the honest case only: suppressed on a stale fallback (one surviving pick there is *not* honest silence), on a stay-home pick (that pick is already the message), and on an aurora pick (no colour-rated alternative to compare against).
- Tests: 4 new `DailyBriefing.test.jsx` cases pinning the note's presence on a fresh single pick and its absence in each of the three suppressed cases (119 in the file, green; ESLint clean). Backend compile/checkstyle clean, `BriefingBestBetAdvisorTest` green.

### Changed — Shared table scaffolding and briefing display vocabulary (frontend)
- `useSortAndFilter` was re-declared file-locally in 3 admin views and `SortableHeader` in 4, and the copies had drifted: only the Location copy supported array (multi-select) filters, only the Tide copy guarded against filter-less columns, and the Tide copy lacked boolean sorting. Promoted the supersets to `hooks/useSortAndFilter.js` and `components/shared/SortableHeader.jsx` and repointed User/Tide/Location/Region. Behaviour-preserving by construction: each view gains only capabilities it didn't exercise, and Region passes `spacer={false}` so its filter-less table keeps its exact header height.
- DailyBriefing and HeatmapGrid shared their display vocabulary by copy (HeatmapGrid's own comment admitted it: "copied from DailyBriefing — shared logic"): byte-identical `VerdictPill` — plus two inline copies of the verdict→display mapping that `tierUtils.resolveRegionDisplay` already owned — `VERDICT_ORDER`, `isPoorSlot`, the weather-code emoji ladder, `msToMph`, `formatDriveDuration`, `formatTime`, `getEventTime`/`isEventPast`, `LOCATION_TYPE_ICONS`. Extracted `components/shared/VerdictPill.jsx` (signal resolution delegates to `resolveRegionDisplay`, so the mapping now has exactly one home) and `utils/briefingDisplay.js`. The two files' slot *orderings* are deliberately NOT merged — the Plan tab sorts verdict-then-A–Z while the heatmap drill-down prioritises king-tide/tide-aligned slots — both are exported explicitly (`sortedSlotsByVerdict` / `sortedSlotsByTidePriority`) so the difference reads as product intent rather than silent drift. `msToMph`/`formatDriveDuration` deliberately stay separate from `conversions.mpsToMph`/`formatDuration` (different precision and units; documented in the module).
- Deleted dead code: DailyBriefing's never-rendered `HeatmapDrillDown` copy (the live one is HeatmapGrid's; it sat under an `eslint-disable no-unused-vars`) and its now-orphaned `getShortDate`.
- Net −705 lines across the six views. The shared modules carry proper PropTypes (the local copies sat under `eslint-disable react/prop-types`). New `briefingDisplay.test.js` (29 tests) pins the two orderings' difference, `isPoorSlot`'s displayVerdict-wins contract, the weather-code boundaries, and the afterglow window; new `SortableHeader.test.jsx` + `VerdictPill.test.jsx` (14 tests) pin the shared components — an adversarial review pass flagged the new `spacer` prop as the only thing preserving pixel parity in two directions yet untested, and its contract is now pinned in both modes. Full frontend suite green (1711 tests, 76 files), ESLint clean.

### Changed — One eligibility policy and one stability-snapshot producer for both forecast engines
- The synchronous engine (`ForecastCommandExecutor`, reachable via the admin "Run Forecast" endpoints) and the production batch pipeline each carried their own copy of two policies, and the copies disagreed. Eligibility: the executor's `applyStabilityFilter` read `ForecastStability.evaluationWindowDays()` as policy (TRANSITIONAL→T+1, UNSETTLED→T+0) while the batch's `NightlyEligibilityPolicy` evaluates TRANSITIONAL to T+2 and UNSETTLED to T+1 — so a scheduled sync run would evaluate a *different slot set* than the nightly batch for the same day. Snapshot: the grid-cell classification + `stability_snapshot` build/publish logic existed in both engines; the duplication already caused one production incident (the stranded snapshot write that collapsed the briefing stability gate to UNSETTLED, documented in `GridCellStabilityService`'s javadoc).
- Unified both: `applyStabilityFilter` now delegates the include/skip decision to `NightlyEligibilityPolicy.permitsHorizon()` (a boolean view of `resolve()`, so the Gate 4 table has exactly one home) and classification/publishing to a shared `GridCellStabilityService` (new `ForecastPreEvalResult` overload; the snapshot schema and publish rule live in one private helper). Two side-effects, both improvements: unclassifiable tasks now fall back to TRANSITIONAL exactly like the batch path (previously a hardcoded T+1 window), and a sync run that classifies zero cells no longer overwrites the authoritative snapshot with an empty one. No production behaviour change — the stability filter only runs on non-manual executions, which currently have no live trigger (admin runs bypass it by design, and the `@Scheduled` crons were retired in v2.12).
- Deleted the four dormant `ScheduledForecastService` methods (`runNearTermForecasts`, `runDistantForecasts`, `runWeatherForecasts`, `refreshExchangeRate`) whose `@Scheduled` annotations were commented out in v2.12 and whose only callers were their own tests, plus the service's now-unused command-factory/executor/exchange-rate dependencies. `ForecastStability.evaluationWindowDays()` is now display-only everywhere; the CLAUDE.md "open inconsistency" note is closed.
- Tests: executor stability-filter tests re-pinned to the unified Gate 4 table with explicit boundaries (UNSETTLED T+1/T+2, TRANSITIONAL T+2/T+3, SETTLED T+3/T+4, no-grid-cell TRANSITIONAL fallback); new `NightlyEligibilityPolicyTest` (boundary rows + permitsHorizon↔resolve agreement sweep) and `GridCellStabilityServiceTest` (sync-path overload: once-per-cell classification, null-response handling, no-publish-on-empty).

### Fixed — Refresh-token rotation race (TOCTOU) closed; reuse now revokes the whole token family
- `AuthenticationService.refresh` checked `isRevoked()`/expiry in Java and then revoked via read-then-save, so two concurrent refreshes presenting the same token could both pass the check and both be issued fresh token pairs — an attacker holding a stolen refresh token could race the victim's legitimate refresh and keep a live token chain without any reuse signal firing. (The race predates the service extraction below; the old inline `AuthController` flow was identical.)
- The revoke is now a database-level compare-and-set (`RefreshTokenRepository.revokeIfActive`: `UPDATE … SET revoked = true WHERE token_hash = ? AND revoked = false`); only the caller that gets an update count of 1 is issued new tokens, so exactly one of any set of concurrent refreshes can win. No schema change — the predicate rides on the existing `revoked` column.
- Reuse-detection hardening on top: presenting an already-revoked token, or losing the compare-and-set race, is treated as evidence of token theft and revokes **all** of that user's active refresh tokens (`revokeAllActiveByUserId`, logged at WARN), forcing every session to re-authenticate. Both paths return the same generic "invalid or expired" message so token state isn't leaked.
- The endpoint's response shape and error messages are unchanged. `AuthenticationServiceTest.Refresh` grew to 7 tests covering both race paths (revoked-at-read → family revoke; compare-and-set count 0 → family revoke, nothing issued) and asserting the expired/disabled/missing-user paths never consume the token; `AuthControllerTest` gained an endpoint-level concurrent-consumption test.

### Fixed — Database connections no longer held across external HTTP/Claude calls; refresh-token rotation is atomic
- Three service flows wrapped slow external calls in `@Transactional` boundaries, pinning one of HikariCP's (default 10) pooled connections for the full duration of the call — a connection-starvation risk under concurrency. `AuroraForecastRunService.runForecast` (PRO-triggerable) held a connection across the NOAA fetch **and one blocking Claude call per requested night**, and a failure on a later night rolled back earlier nights' already-paid-for results plus the upfront delete. `DriveDurationService.refreshForUser` acquired its 2-permit ORS semaphore *inside* the transaction — threads queueing for the semaphore each pinned a connection. `UserSettingsService` was class-level `@Transactional`, wrapping postcodes.io geocoding lookups.
- Restructured all three to the write-isolation pattern the codebase already uses (`ForecastScoreWriter`): external work runs outside any transaction, and persistence happens in new short-transaction writers — `AuroraForecastResultWriter.replaceNightResults` (per-night atomic delete+insert, so a re-run failure leaves the previous night's results intact and earlier nights stay committed) and `UserDriveTimeWriter.replaceForUser` (atomic replace, so a failed write no longer wipes a user's drive times). `UserSettingsService` keeps a transaction only on `saveHome`.
- Extracted `AuthenticationService` from `AuthController` (which held the full login/refresh/issuance flows inline): refresh-token **rotation is now one transaction** — previously revoke-old and save-new were two independent implicit transactions, so a failure between them stranded the user with no valid refresh token. `login`/`changePassword` are deliberately *not* transactional — the ~100 ms BCrypt check/encode runs with no pooled connection held (an adversarial review pass caught the first cut wrapping BCrypt in the login transaction, reintroducing the very anti-pattern on a public endpoint; their writes are independent single-row saves, so nothing is lost). The token-issuance block duplicated between `login` and `set-password` is now one method; the password complexity policy moved to the service (with pre-compiled patterns instead of per-call `Pattern.compile`). API response shapes are byte-identical; all 39 `AuthControllerTest` MockMvc tests pass unchanged.
- New unit tests: `AuthenticationServiceTest` (21 tests: login/refresh/rotation/revoke/complexity boundaries), `AuroraForecastResultWriterTest`, `UserDriveTimeWriterTest`; `AuroraForecastRunServiceTest` now pins the per-night replace on **all four** outcome branches plus the headline failure-isolation semantics (a two-night run where Claude fails on night 2 must commit night 1 and leave night 2's stored rows untouched). Full backend suite green (the 5 Docker-dependent integration tests need CI), checkstyle 0, SpotBugs 0, JaCoCo per-class gate passing.

## [v2.15.5] - 2026-07-15

### Fixed — Dependabot auto-merge no longer dies on the approve step
- Every Dependabot PR since the workflow landed stopped at `gh pr review --approve`, which fails with "GitHub Actions is not permitted to approve pull requests" unless the repo opts Actions into approving. The step exiting non-zero killed the job before `gh pr merge --auto` ran, so six green dev-dependency bumps (#232–#237) sat unmerged rather than merging themselves.
- Removed the approve step outright rather than enabling the repo setting: `main`'s protection requires the two CI checks and **no** approving review, so the approval satisfied no rule — it was ceremony that only ever failed. `gh pr merge --auto --squash` still gates on the required checks, so this cannot merge a red PR.

### Changed — One definition of the 75% clear/overcast boundary (`CloudScoringRules`)
- The cloud percentage at which a sky stops counting as "clear" was defined **six times** under three different names: `CLEAR_SKY_THRESHOLD` (`MeteorClarityService`, `NlcClarityService`, `BriefingAuroraSummaryBuilder`), `OVERCAST_THRESHOLD_PERCENT` (`WeatherTriageService`), and `DEFAULT_OVERCAST_PERCENT` (`NorthwardTransectSampler`, `OverheadCloudSampler`). Changing the rule meant editing six constants — miss one and a location counts as clear for the meteor pill but overcast for aurora triage.
- Extracted `CloudScoringRules` (`OVERCAST_PERCENT` + `isClear(cloudPercent)`) and repointed all six. Every site already used the identical `cloud < 75` test, and the three fail-open sites (a failed cloud fetch assumes the overcast boundary, so a fetch problem quietly suppresses a "clear" count rather than inventing a clear sky) now share the same constant — so this is a pure de-duplication with no behaviour change. The two unrelated `75`s (moon illumination in `AstroConditionsService` and the aurora prompt) are deliberately left independent.

### Changed — Named `PromptBuilder`'s strip-detection thresholds; de-duplicated its thin-strip rule
- The strip-vs-blanket block carried inline `80`/`50`/`30`, and the thin-strip condition (`low ≥ 50` and a `≥ 30`pp drop to the far-field sample) was written out twice — once to label the directional-cloud block and again for the cloud-approach wording, so the two could silently diverge. Named the thresholds (`SOLAR_MID_CLOUD_THICK_PERCENT`, `SOLAR_LOW_CLOUD_SIGNIFICANT_PERCENT`, `THIN_STRIP_DROP_POINTS`) and folded both copies into a single `isThinStrip(...)`.
- The `113km`/`226km` distances quoted to Claude are now derived from `DirectionalSamplingGeometry`'s sampling constants, so the distance in the prompt can't drift from the distance actually sampled. Prompt output is byte-identical (the prompt tests assert it unchanged).

### Changed — ADMIN metrics/model-test/prompt-test endpoints return DTOs, not raw JPA entities
- Four ADMIN controllers (`JobMetricsController`, `ModelTestController`, `PromptTestController`, `BriefingController`'s compare-models) serialized raw JPA entities straight from the repositories, leaking internal columns and lazy `@OneToMany` relations onto the API. Introduced 8 response DTOs (records in `model/` with a `from(entity)` factory) exposing only the fields the frontend and tests actually consume, and repointed the endpoints.
- The most notable leaks closed: `api_call_log.request_url` (the full outbound URL **with query parameters** — potential secret/API-key exposure) and `request_body` (raw request payloads); the large `prompt_sent`/`response_json`/`raw_response` reproduction TEXT blobs on test-result rows; and the `apiCalls`/`results` lazy child collections (which risked `LazyInitializationException`/N+1 and dumped the whole child graph — the children have their own endpoints).
- Each DTO is drop-in (same JSON field names, a subset), so the frontend and the controller tests are unchanged. Verified: the DTO field set was derived per-entity from the actual frontend reads ∪ test assertions (so nothing the UI reads was dropped — independently re-audited), full backend suite green (4416 tests), SpotBugs 0, checkstyle clean, and all 8 new DTOs at 100% line coverage.

### Changed — Dropped `OpenMeteoService`'s test-only delegators; tests call the collaborators directly
- The `OpenMeteoService` decomposition left thin pass-through delegators so the existing tests kept compiling. Removed the eight that were reached **only** by tests (`computeSolarHorizonPoint`, the five `extract*` methods, `findBestIndex`, `findNearestIndex`) and repointed `OpenMeteoServiceTest` straight at `DirectionalSamplingGeometry` / `OpenMeteoResponseParser` — so the extraction/geometry tests now exercise the class that actually owns the logic. `OpenMeteoService` sheds ~135 lines of redundant pass-throughs; no behaviour change.
- **Deliberately kept** the five delegators that other production classes call (`computeDirectionalCloudPoints`, `computeUpwindPoint`, and the three `*FromCache` methods — used by `ForecastService`, `ForecastCommandExecutor`, `ForecastDataAugmentor`, `BatchWeatherPrefetcher`). Those are a genuine facade: dropping them would push the collaborator imports out into four more services, spreading coupling rather than reducing it.

### Changed — Moved the last direct-axios component calls onto the API layer
- Four components (`LocationAlerts`, `ManageView`, `UserManagementView`, `WaitlistManagementView`) still called `apiClient` directly instead of going through an `api/*.js` module — the tail end of the axios-client consolidation. Added `getUsers`/`createUser` to `userApi.js`, a new `waitlistApi.js` (`getWaitlist`), and `resetLocationFailures` to `forecastApi.js`, and repointed all four components. Component tests now mock the api modules rather than the transport. No behaviour change; full frontend suite green (1664 tests), ESLint 0 errors, build clean.

### Fixed — Briefing-gloss and best-bet Anthropic calls now record their real token cost
- `JobRunService.logApiCall`'s generic path hardcoded `costMicroDollars = 0` for `ANTHROPIC` (it has no token usage). Three callers use that generic path for Anthropic deliberately — they need to log a custom request URL + request body for the replay harness, which the token-based `logAnthropicApiCall` can't — so the two success-path calls (`briefing-best-bet` and `briefing-gloss`) were logging **£0**, under-reporting job-run spend.
- Added a token-aware `logApiCall` overload that computes the real micro-dollar cost from the call's `TokenUsage` (and stores the token columns), and repointed those two callers to extract usage from their `Message` response. The failed-call error path is left at 0 (correct — no usage). No behaviour change for any non-Anthropic caller.

### Changed — De-duplicated the directional-cloud sampling geometry in `OpenMeteoService`
- `fetchDirectionalCloudData` and `fetchCloudApproachData` re-built their sampling points inline instead of using the already-extracted `DirectionalSamplingGeometry`. `fetchDirectionalCloudData` now calls `computeDirectionalCloudPoints` (with a named `SOLAR_CONE_POINT_COUNT` constant for the averaging divisor) and `fetchCloudApproachData`'s solar point uses `computeSolarHorizonPoint`. Behaviour-identical (verified byte-for-byte and by the full `OpenMeteoServiceTest`); the upwind sample stays inline since it needs the sampling distance the geometry helper doesn't return.

### Fixed — Map no longer skips a re-fit when region and focus handoffs share a nonce
- `MapView`'s `FitBoundsController` re-fits the map only when its handoff `key` changes, but two independent sources wrote that key from separate monotonic nonce counters — the Plan-tab **region** handoff and the map-overlay **focus** — using the bare integer. Because both counters start low and increment independently, they could land on the same integer in succession (e.g. first region tap → `1`, then first focus overlay → `1`), leaving the key unchanged so the map silently kept the previous fit instead of framing the new pins.
- Namespaced the key by source via a small `fitBoundsKey(source, nonce)` helper (`region:1` vs `focus:1`), so equal nonces from different sources can never collide. Behaviour changes only in the collision case (the map now correctly re-fits). Found by an audit of the `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` suppressions — the dependency arrays themselves were sound (the bug was the shared integer key space, not a stale closure). Unit-tested via `fitBoundsKey.test.js`.

### Changed — Frontend API layer consolidated onto one shared axios client
- The JWT request interceptor and the 401-refresh response interceptor used to be registered on the global `axios` singleton as an import side effect of `forecastApi.js`, and the other ~25 `api/*.js` modules (plus four components) relied on that global mutation being in place — accidental, import-order-dependent coupling. Introduced `src/api/axiosClient.js`, a dedicated `axios.create()` instance that owns both interceptors; every module now imports it explicitly.
- Behaviour is identical (same token attach, same single-flight 401 refresh + `session-expired` handling — the interceptors were moved verbatim, only the retry target changed from the global to the instance). Repointed 21 API modules + 4 components off the global axios; the two non-axios modules (`fetch`/`EventSource`) and `geocodePlace`'s deliberate interceptor-free `fetch` were left as-is. Verified with the full frontend suite (1660 tests), ESLint (0 errors), and a production build (confirming the `axiosClient`↔`authApi` import cycle resolves cleanly).

## [v2.15.4] - 2026-07-13

### Changed — Decomposed `ForecastTaskCollector` (SRP; 1317 → 785 lines)
- The last and trickiest of the three oversized-service decompositions. `ForecastTaskCollector` mixed briefing candidate collection, weather/cloud pre-fetch, grid-cell stability classification, force-eval headline selection, and per-task assembly. Extracted the four cohesive seams into instance collaborators constructed inside the collector's constructor from its existing dependency fields — so the collector's public constructor and the ~80 tests' `verify(dep)`/`verifyNoInteractions(dep)` assertions (which hinge on the injected mock instances) keep working unchanged.
- **`BriefingCandidateCollector`** (new) — the first pass over the briefing applying the cheap gates (past-date, travel-day, region-cache freshness, verdict/hard-constraint, unknown-location); now a pure producer returning a `{candidates, dispositions}` record instead of mutating a passed-in list.
- **`BatchWeatherPrefetcher`** (new) — bulk Open-Meteo weather + directional/upwind cloud-point pre-fetch (pure data assembly).
- **`ForceEvalHeadlineSelector`** (new) — scans the briefing for far-out GO cells and picks the capped force-eval key set that rescues headline contenders past the stability gate.
- **`GridCellStabilityService`** (new) — classifies each grid cell and publishes the stability snapshot; its `stabilityFor` preserves the exact shared-state contract (the `stabilityByCell` map stays a local owned by the triage loop and passed in, lazily mutated via `computeIfAbsent` — never hidden as a field).
- Promoted the private `ForecastCandidate` record to a package-private top-level type shared by every seam. Added a focused `BatchWeatherPrefetcherTest` for the upwind wind-sampling branch the collector-level tests never exercised (that under-tested code, previously masked in the large class, dropped below the 80% per-class gate once isolated).
- Behaviour-preserving: no production logic changed, only relocated — full suite green (4414 tests, 0 failures; SpotBugs 0) and a 4-agent byte-exact review of the candidate gates, force-eval ranking, shared-state threading, and constructor wiring returned zero findings. **Completes the oversized-service cleanup** (all three of `OpenMeteoService`, `BriefingBestBetAdvisor`, `ForecastTaskCollector` now decomposed).

### Changed — Decomposed `BriefingBestBetAdvisor`, phase 2 (SRP; 1241 → 594 lines)
- Completes the `BriefingBestBetAdvisor` decomposition begun in phase 1 by extracting the three **dependency-heavy** seams. Because these need injected collaborators, they are instance classes constructed inside the advisor's constructor from its existing dependency fields — so the advisor's public constructor is unchanged and the ~140-test suite still needs no edits.
- **`BriefingRollupBuilder`** (new) — assembles the compact region-level rollup JSON (events, regions, tide/stability/Claude-score fields, the aurora block) and derives the `RollupResult` validation sets. The largest seam (~460 lines, 7 deps); it composes `AuroraRegionSelector` for the aurora block.
- **`AuroraRegionSelector`** (new) — derives the best dark-sky region for tonight's aurora from cached scores (rank by clear count, mean stars, darkness).
- **`BestBetEnricher`** (new) — adds display-only structured fields (dayName / eventType / eventTime, incl. Today/Tomorrow labelling) to picks from the triage data.
- Promoted the `RollupResult` record to a top-level `model` record (the contract between the rollup builder and all three call paths). Across both phases **`BriefingBestBetAdvisor` shrank 1888 → 594 lines (−69%)**; the advisor is now a thin orchestration layer delegating to seven focused collaborators.
- Behaviour-preserving: no production logic changed, only relocated — verified by the full suite (4412 tests, 0 failures; SpotBugs 0) and a 4-agent byte-exact review of the JSON assembly, aurora ranking, enrichment, and constructor wiring (zero findings). New classes clear the 80% per-class coverage gate (rollup builder 99%, aurora selector 100%, enricher 88%).

### Changed — Decomposed `BriefingBestBetAdvisor`, phase 1 (SRP; 1888 → 1241 lines)
- `BriefingBestBetAdvisor` had grown to 1888 lines running the whole best-bet pipeline (Anthropic orchestration, rollup-JSON assembly, aurora selection, Claude-response parsing, pick validation, coverage-aware ranking, display enrichment, and a ~260-line system prompt). Phase 1 extracts the four **pure, low-risk** seams into focused stateless collaborators; the advisor keeps every method as a thin delegator, so its public API and constructor are unchanged and the ~130-test suite needed no assertion edits.
- **`BestBetPromptText`** (new) — holds the ~260-line `SYSTEM_PROMPT` text block (≈14% of the old file); `currentSystemPrompt()` still returns it for the replay harness.
- **`BestBetResponseParser`** (new) — parses raw Claude text into a `BestBetResult`, classifies the outcome, and salvages complete leading picks from a truncated response (`ObjectMapper` passed as a parameter).
- **`BestBetPickValidator`** (new) — filters parsed picks against the rollup's valid event/region/day-name sets.
- **`BestBetRanker`** (new) — coverage-aware headline gating, colour-exempt handling, and inter-pick relationship/`differsBy` derivation.
- Promoted the `CandidateCoverage` record from a package-private nested type to a top-level `model` record (shared between the ranker and the still-in-place rollup builder). `BestBetPromptText` is added to the JaCoCo per-class exclude list (a compile-time-constant holder, never loaded — matching the repo's convention for no-logic classes).
- Behaviour-preserving: no production logic changed, only relocated — verified by the full suite (4412 tests, 0 failures) and a 4-agent byte-exact review (zero findings). Phase 2 (follow-up) will extract the dependency-heavy seams (`AuroraRegionSelector`, the 460-line `BriefingRollupBuilder`, `BestBetEnricher`). Second of three planned oversized-service decompositions.

### Changed — Decomposed `OpenMeteoService` (SRP; 1169 → 851 lines)
- `OpenMeteoService` had grown to 1169 lines mixing seven concerns (HTTP orchestration, telemetry, response parsing, trend derivation, sampling geometry, cache re-extraction, URL/coord-key formatting). Extracted the three pure, cohesive seams into focused stateless collaborators, leaving the service as an orchestration facade that delegates to them. **Public API unchanged** — all seven callers (`ForecastService`, `ForecastCommandExecutor`, `ForecastDataAugmentor`, `ModelTestService`, `PromptTestService`, `AstroConditionsService`, `ForecastTaskCollector`) are untouched.
- **`DirectionalSamplingGeometry`** (new) — the solar-cone / antisolar / far-solar / upwind lat-lon sampling math (`computeDirectionalCloudPoints`, `computeSolarHorizonPoint`, `computeUpwindPoint`) plus its distance/cone constants. Pure functions over `GeoUtils`, matching the existing `GeoUtils`/`TimeSlotUtils` utility pattern.
- **`OpenMeteoResponseParser`** (new) — raw forecast/air-quality responses → domain records (`extractAtmosphericData`, `extractMistTrend`/`extractPressureTrend`/`extractSolarTrend`/`extractUpwindSample`, the numeric guards, `findNearestIndex`) with the slot-selection, `HALF_UP` rounding and null-guard rules.
- **`CloudPointCacheReader`** (new) — no-API re-extraction of directional-cloud and cloud-approach data from a prefetched `CloudPointCache`, composing the geometry + parser seams.
- Behaviour-preserving: the ~96-test `OpenMeteoServiceTest` exercises the moved logic through the unchanged delegators and stays green. Added 4 characterization tests for `prefetchWeatherBatchResilient` (empty-coords guard, total-forecast-failure short-circuit, partial-forecast null-skip, air-quality-failure fallback) — the one method the file previously left uncovered. First of three planned oversized-service decompositions.

### Changed — Standardised RestClient mocking in tests (`RestClientMocks` helper)
- The test suite mocked Spring's `RestClient` fluent chain inconsistently — some files hand-rolled 4–5 `RequestHeadersUriSpec`/`ResponseSpec` mocks (often behind a per-file `mockRestClientGet`/`setupPostChain`/`stubRestClientBody` clone), others used `mock(RestClient.class, RETURNS_DEEP_STUBS)` one-liners. Extracted a single **`com.gregochr.goldenhour.util.RestClientMocks`** test helper with `stubGet`/`stubGetSequence`/`stubGetThrows`/`stubGetBodilessEntity`/`stubGetBodilessEntityThrows`/`stubPost`/`stubPostThrows`, each stubbing the whole chain in one call.
- The helper covers all five `uri(...)` overloads plus GET `headers(...)` and POST `contentType`/`header`/`body` via lenient *plumbing* stubs, leaving the terminal `body(Class)`/`toBodilessEntity()` strict — so a wrong body-type or unreached stub fails **loud** (`UnnecessaryStubbingException`/NPE), never a silent vacuous pass. This structural leniency is distinct from the behavioural `lenient()` smell the test standards target.
- **Migrated 13 canned-response files** (clients, health indicators, `TurnstileService`, `ExchangeRateService`, `TideService`, …) to the helper, deleting their bespoke chain-building helpers and now-unused Mockito imports (including two stray `lenient()` stubs).
- **Migrated the two request-asserting tests to `MockRestServiceServer`**: `AuroraWeatherEnricherTest` (asserts `wind_speed_unit=ms` / `forecast_days=3` query params — replacing an awkward URI-capture `thenAnswer`) and `PushoverNotificationServiceTest` (asserts the JSON body via `jsonPath` — replacing `ArgumentCaptor<Map>` boilerplate). Every prior assertion is preserved.
- Left on explicit mocking by design (not canned-response cases): the six `OpenMeteoClientTest` retry tests (interleaved `thenThrow`/`thenReturn` sequences + `verify(times(2))` interaction checks) and `LocationEnrichmentServiceTest` (asserts exact lat/lon in the elevation URL via its own helpers). No production code changed; full suite green (4404 tests, 0 failures).

### Removed — Legacy pence-based cost tracking (micro-dollars is now the single source of truth)
- Ripped out the deprecated flat-rate **pence** cost system that ran in parallel with token-based **micro-dollar** pricing. Since the V38 token-cost migration every record has carried `cost_micro_dollars`/`total_cost_micro_dollars` (+ the stored exchange rate for GBP), and the frontend already rendered GBP from micro-dollars, falling back to pence only for pre-token rows — so the pence path was dead for all current data and only added dual-system confusion.
- **Backend:** deleted the two `@Deprecated CostCalculator.calculateCost(...)` overloads and the five `CostProperties` pence fields; removed every pence write and the `completeRun` pence aggregation from `JobRunService`, `ModelTestService`, and `PromptTestService` (their `completeRun` helpers lost the `totalCostPence` param); dropped the deprecated `cost.*-pence` keys from the `example`/`local`/`prod` YAML profiles (token rates fall back to `CostProperties` defaults).
- **Schema:** migration **V125** drops the six now-unused columns — `api_call_log.cost_pence`, `job_run.total_cost_pence`, `model_test_run.total_cost_pence`, `model_test_result.cost_pence`, `prompt_test_run.total_cost_pence`, `prompt_test_result.cost_pence` — and the matching `@Column` fields were removed from the entities (they were serialized raw on the ADMIN metrics/test endpoints, so this also drops them from those responses; no DTO change needed).
- **Frontend:** `formatCostGbp(microDollars, exchangeRate)` lost its legacy-pence third argument and fallback branch; `MetricsSummary` lost its `legacyOnlyPence`/`hasMixedPricing` logic and mixed-pricing copy (cost is now always "Token-based pricing"); `JobRunDetail`, `JobRunsGrid`, `ModelTestView`, `PromptTestView`, and `BriefingModelTestView` dropped their pence reads and the stale third arg.
- **Behaviour change (accepted, admin-only):** pre-V38 historical metrics/model-test/prompt-test rows — which never had micro-dollar costs — now render "—" instead of a legacy pence estimate. No LITE/PRO forecast data is affected (`ForecastEvaluationDto` never carried cost fields).

### Added — Meteor pill gains an overhead clear-sky count ("clear at X of Y dark-sky locations")
- Completes the "clear at X of Y" idiom across all three night topics. The meteor pill now carries a fact showing how many dark-sky locations are forecast clear on the shower peak night — e.g. `Perseids peak — dark moon, good viewing · ZHR ~100 at peak · radiant best 01:00–04:00 NE · clear at 12 of 30 dark-sky locations`.
- **Honest whole-sky signal, not the horizon proxy.** Aurora and NLC count "clear" from a northern-horizon transect because those phenomena sit low on the poleward horizon. A meteor shower is whole-sky, so a new `OverheadCloudSampler` measures total-column cloud (low+mid+high, capped) **directly above each dark-sky site** — a location under a clear northern horizon but cloud overhead is honestly counted as *not* clear.
- **No API call from the strategy.** Mirroring the NLC pattern, a new `MeteorClarityService` runs the scan during the daily-briefing run (`BriefingService`, failure-isolated) into an in-memory cache the read-only strategy consumes. It fetches **only on shower-peak nights** (gated by the shared `MeteorHotTopicStrategy.peakDatesWithin`), so the vast majority of briefings do no meteor cloud fetch at all. Each peak night is sampled at its deep-night hour; the count is omitted entirely (never fabricated) if the scan didn't run or returned an inconsistent count.
- Frontend needs no change — the fact renders through the generic `TopicFacts` line. Tests: `OverheadCloudSamplerTest` (at-location coords vs the northward transect, `SUM_CAPPED`, lenient overcast defaults, grid dedup), `MeteorClarityServiceTest` (peak-gating with no fetch off-peak, the 75% clear boundary, empty dark-sky), and `MeteorHotTopicStrategyTest` (the fact present/absent/guarded, `peakDatesWithin`).

### Changed — Aurora pill adopts the same "clear at X of Y dark-sky locations" idiom
